.

IV.3.3
Condensable Algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}

In this case,
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}
is again the toric code

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\mathcal{Z}/{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}=\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})=\{1,e,m,f\}\,.

(IV.122)

A Lagrangian algebra in
\mathcal{Z}(S_{3})\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}
which completes
{\mathcal{A}}_{E}
is

\mathcal{L}_{I}=1\oplus a_{1}\oplus 1_{-}\overline{e}\oplus a_{1}\overline{e}\oplus b_{+}\overline{m}\oplus b_{-}\overline{f}\,.

(IV.123)

The Lagrangian algebra for the underlying non-symmetric gapped boundary phase is obtained easily to be

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}=1\oplus e\,.

(IV.124)

This implies that the underlying boundary of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
DW theory is irreducible

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}^{e}\,,

(IV.125)

and associated to Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{e}=1\oplus e
. The topological lines on the boundary are

1,\quad P\,,

(IV.126)

where
P
generates the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry localized on the boundary.

Here the club quiche is simply

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}})
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}}}
{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}
1_{-}
e
=
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
1\oplus e
{\mathcal{E}}_{-}
e

(IV.127)

with the action of
S_{3}
being

a:~{\mathcal{E}}_{-}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{-},\qquad b:~{\mathcal{E}}_{-}\to-{\mathcal{E}}_{-}

(IV.128)

from which we identify the lines

\phi(b)=P\,,\qquad\phi(a)=1

(IV.129)

to be the generators of the
S_{3}
symmetry along
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
.

Mathematically, we have provided a pivotal tensor functor

\phi:~\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}}\to\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}

(IV.130)

where
\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
is the fusion category formed by topological lines living on the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
. All the data of this functor simply descends from the non-trivial homomorphism
S_{3}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
that measures the (parity of the) number of transpositions involved in a permutation of 3 objects.

IV.4
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
Symmetry

We now consider minimal (1+1)d
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped boundary phases. In this case we have

{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})=\{1,1_{-},E\}\,,

(IV.131)

where
1_{-}
denotes the 1d sign irreducible representation of
S_{3}
and
E
denotes the 2d irreducible representation of
S_{3}
. The fusion rules are

1_{-}\otimes E=E\otimes 1_{-}=E\,,\qquad E^{2}=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus E\,.

(IV.132)

Recall that
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
can be obtained by gauging
S_{3}
symmetry, which implies the two symmetries have the same SymTFT

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3}))=\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}})\,.

(IV.133)

The difference lies in the choice of the symmetry boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}
, which for
{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
we take to be specified by the Lagrangian algebra

\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})}=1\oplus a_{1}\oplus b_{+}\,.

(IV.134)

Since the SymTFTs are the same, the non-trivial condensable algebras remain the same
{\mathcal{A}}_{E}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{1_{-}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}
we discussed above. Each of these choices defines a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped boundary, analogously to the
\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}}
case. However, the fact that the symmetry boundary is now associated to
\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})}
implies that the results are different to what we obtained before. We now discuss them in turn.

IV.4.1
Condensable algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{E}

In this case, as we discussed earlier, we have that
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}
is the toric code, and a Lagrangian algebra in
\mathcal{Z}(S_{3})\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}
which completes
{\mathcal{A}}_{E}
was provided in (
IV.107
). Here, we instead work with a different but equivalent Lagrangian algebra

\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}=1\oplus E\oplus 1_{-}\overline{m}\oplus E\overline{m}\oplus b_{-}\overline{f}\oplus b_{+}\overline{e}\,.

(IV.135)

obtained from (
IV.107
) by applying the
em
-duality symmetry of the toric code
7
7

7

This is done just to ensure that the underlying boundary of the resulting gapped phase would still be the electric boundary (or a multiple of it) of the toric code. Working instead with (
IV.107
) produces same results with the underlying boundary being the magnetic one.
.

The Lagrangian algebra associated to the underlying non-symmetric gapped boundary phase is

\mathcal{L}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}}=1\oplus e=\mathcal{L}_{e}\,,

(IV.136)

which means that
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is an irreducible topological boundary
associated to electric Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{e}
. The topological lines on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
are as in (
IV.126
).

The club quiche is:

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3}))
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})}
{\mathcal{A}}_{E}
b_{+}
e
=
e
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}_{e}
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}

(IV.137)

The
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
generators linking action descends from the corresponding action on the ends of the
\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}})
anyons and is given by

1_{-}:\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{E}}_{e}&\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{e},\\
\text{id}&\to\text{id},\end{aligned}\qquad\quad E:\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{E}}_{e}&\rightarrow 0,\\
\text{id}&\to 2\,\text{id}\,.\end{aligned}

(IV.138)

where
id
denotes the identity local operator along
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
. See
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
for more details. Comparing with the action of lines living on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
, we find that the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry is realized on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
by lines

\phi(1_{-})=P\quad,\quad\phi(E)=1\oplus P\,.

(IV.139)

It is easy to verify that the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
fusion rules are satisfied

\displaystyle\phi(1_{-})\phi(E)=\phi(E)\phi(1_{-})=P\otimes(1\oplus P)=1\oplus P=\phi(E)\,,

(IV.140)

\displaystyle[\phi(E)]^{2}=(1\oplus P)^{2}=2(1\oplus P)=\phi(1)\oplus\phi(1_{-})\oplus\phi(E)\,.

Mathematically, we have provided a pivotal tensor functor

\phi:~\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})\to\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}

(IV.141)

where
\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
is the fusion category formed by topological lines living on the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
.

IV.4.2
Condensable algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{1_{-}}

In this case, as we discussed earlier, we have that
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}
is the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{3}
DW theory, and a Lagrangian algebra completing

was provided in (

). Here, we instead work with a different but equivalent Lagrangian algebra

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{{\mathcal{I}}}=

(IV.142)

\displaystyle\oplus a_{\omega^{2}}\overline{e}^{2}\overline{m}^{2}\oplus E\overline{m}\oplus E\overline{m}^{2}\oplus a_{\omega}\overline{e}^{2}\overline{m}\oplus a_{\omega}\overline{e}\overline{m}^{2}\,.

obtained from (
IV.90
) by applying the
em
exchange duality.

From this we see that the Lagrangian algebra associated to the underlying non-symmetric gapped boundary phase is

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}=1\oplus e\oplus e^{2}=\mathcal{L}_{e}\,,

(IV.143)

which means that
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is an irreducible topological boundary
associated to electric Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{e}
. The topological lines on

are

1,\qquad P,\qquad P^{2}\,,

(IV.144)

where

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
.

The operators coming from the club quiche are:

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3}))
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{3}})

The

1_{-}:\quad\begin{aligned} \text{id}&\to\text{id}\cr{\mathcal{E}}_{e}&\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{e}\cr{\mathcal{E}}_{e^{2}}&\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{e^{2}}\end{aligned}\qquad E:\quad\begin{aligned} \text{id}&\to 2\,\text{id}\cr{\mathcal{E}}_{e}&\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{e}\cr{\mathcal{E}}_{e^{2}}&\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{e^{2}}\,.\end{aligned}

The reader can easily check that the

Mathematically, we have provided a pivotal tensor functor

\phi:~\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})\to\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}\,,

(IV.148)

where

IV.4.3
Condensable algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}

In this case, we know that
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}
is again the toric code. The Lagrangian algebra in
\mathcal{Z}(S_{3})\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}
which completes
{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}
is described in (
IV.123
).

From this we see that the decomposable algebra associated to the underlying non-symmetric gapped boundary is

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}=(1\oplus e)\oplus(1\oplus m)=\mathcal{L}_{e}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{m}\,,

(IV.149)

i.e.
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is a reducible boundary of the form

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}_{e}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{m}\,,

(IV.150)

where
\mathfrak{B}_{e}
is an irreducible topological boundary with associated Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{e}=1\oplus e
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}
is an irreducible topological boundary with associated Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{m}=1\oplus m
.

The topological line operators on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
are

1_{ii},\qquad P_{ii},\qquad S_{ij}\,,

(IV.151)

where
i,j\in\{e,m\}
and
i\neq j
. See the end of appendix
A
for more details. The line
1_{ii}
is the identity line on boundary
\mathfrak{B}_{i}
, the line
P_{ii}
is the generator of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry localized along the boundary
\mathfrak{B}_{i}
, and the line
S_{ij}
changes the boundary
\mathfrak{B}_{i}
to the boundary
\mathfrak{B}_{j}
, with fusion rules

\displaystyle P_{ii}\otimes S_{ij}=S_{ij}\otimes P_{jj}

\displaystyle=S_{ij}

(IV.152)

\displaystyle S_{ij}\otimes S_{ji}

\displaystyle=1_{ii}\oplus P_{ii}\,.

Note the non-invertibility of the boundary changing line operators
S_{ij}
. Note also that the general linking actions of boundary changing lines are

\displaystyle S_{em}

\displaystyle:~v_{e}\to\sqrt{2}e^{-\lambda}v_{m},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{e}\to 0

(IV.153)

\displaystyle S_{me}

\displaystyle:~v_{m}\to\sqrt{2}e^{\lambda}v_{e},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\to 0

for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
which captures the relative Euler term between the two boundaries
\mathfrak{B}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}
.

The operators coming from the club quiche are

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3}))
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})}
{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}
a_{1}
a_{1}
b_{+}
m
e
=
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}_{e}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{m}
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}
{\mathcal{O}}
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}
e
m

(IV.154)

The products of these operators are fixed to be

\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}^{2}&=2-{\mathcal{O}}\,,\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}&=0\,,\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{E}}_{e}&={\mathcal{E}}_{e}\,,\\
{\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}&=-2{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\,,\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{E}}_{e}^{2}&=(2+{\mathcal{O}})/3\,,\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{2}&=(1-{\mathcal{O}})/3\,.\end{aligned}

(IV.155)

The computation is identical to the one described in section 5.3.4 of
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
where the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
SSB phase is discussed.
The identity operators along the two boundaries
\mathfrak{B}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}
can be identified respectively as

v_{e}=\frac{1}{3}(2+{\mathcal{O}})\,,\qquad v_{m}=\frac{1}{3}(1-{\mathcal{O}})

(IV.156)

The linking actions of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry generators are

\displaystyle 1_{-}

\displaystyle:~1\to 1,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{e}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{e},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{m},

(IV.157)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:~1\to 2,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{O}},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{e}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{e},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\rightarrow 0\,.

where
1=v_{e}+v_{m}
denotes the identity operator along the reducible boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
. This implies the following linking actions

\displaystyle 1_{-}

\displaystyle:~v_{e}\rightarrow v_{e},\qquad\quad\qquad v_{m}\rightarrow v_{m}

(IV.158)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:~v_{e}\rightarrow 2v_{m}+v_{e},\qquad v_{m}\rightarrow v_{e}\,.

Therefore the lines realizing the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry along
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
are

\phi(1_{-})=1_{ee}\oplus P_{mm},\quad\phi(E)=S_{em}\oplus S_{me}\oplus P_{ee}

(IV.159)

with the relative Euler term between
\mathfrak{B}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}
fixed such that the linking actions of boundary changing lines are

S_{em}:~v_{e}\to 2v_{m},\qquad S_{me}:~v_{m}\to v_{e}\,.

(IV.160)

The reader can check that these lines satisfy the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
fusion rules.

Mathematically, we have provided information about a pivotal tensor functor

\phi:~\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})\to\mathsf{Ising}^{\sqrt{2}}_{2\times 2}\,,

(IV.161)

where
\mathsf{Ising}^{e^{-\lambda}}_{2\times 2}
is what we call the pivotal multi-fusion category formed by topological lines (
IV.151
) with the quantum dimensions of
S_{ij}
lines given by their linking actions on
v_{i}
described in (
IV.153
).

IV.5
\mathsf{Ising}
Symmetry

We now study (1+1)d
\mathsf{Ising}
-symmetric minimal gapped boundary phases. This is the smallest Tambara-Yamagami symmetry category
\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})

\mathsf{Ising}=\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})=\{1,P,S\}

(IV.162)

with fusion rules

P^{2}=1\;,\;P\otimes S=S\otimes P=S\;,\;S^{2}=1\oplus P\,.

(IV.163)

The SymTFT
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Ising})
is the doubled Ising theory, whose bulk anyons are

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Ising})=\mathsf{Ising}\boxtimes\overline{\mathsf{Ising}}=\{1,P,\overline{P},P\overline{P},S,\overline{S},P\overline{S},S\overline{P},S\overline{S}\}\,.

(IV.164)

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Ising})
admits only one topological boundary condition corresponding to the Lagrangian algebra

\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Ising}}=1\oplus P\overline{P}\oplus S\overline{S}\,.

(IV.165)

We find only one non-trivial non-Lagrangian condensable algebra at object level that solves the necessary conditions in
B
, given by

{\mathcal{A}}=1\oplus P\overline{P}\,.

(IV.166)

The bulk phase
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}
is the toric code
with the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Ising})\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})}
completing
{\mathcal{A}}
being

\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}=1\oplus P\overline{P}\oplus P\overline{f}\oplus\overline{P}\overline{f}\oplus S\overline{S}\,\overline{e}\oplus S\overline{S}\,\overline{m}\,.

(IV.167)

From this, we find that the decomposable algebra associated to the underlying non-symmetric gapped boundary is

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}=(1\oplus e)\oplus(1\oplus m)=\mathcal{L}_{e}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{m}\,,

(IV.168)

i.e.
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is a reducible boundary of the form

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}_{e}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{m}\,,

(IV.169)

which is the same as in (
IV.150
). The topological line operators on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
are discussed in (
IV.151
), with fusion (
IV.152
), and linking action (
IV.153
) for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
capturing the relative Euler term between
\mathfrak{B}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}
, which will turn out to be different in this case.

The operators we obtain from the club quiche are

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Ising})
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Ising}}
{\mathcal{A}}
S\overline{S}
P\overline{P}
S\overline{S}
e
m
=
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}_{e}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{m}
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}
{\mathcal{O}}
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}
m
e

(IV.170)

The products of these operators are determined to be

\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}^{2}&=1\,,\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}&=0\,,\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{E}}_{e}&={\mathcal{E}}_{e}\,,\\
{\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}&=-{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\,,\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{E}}_{e}^{2}&=(1+{\mathcal{O}})/2\,,\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{2}&=(1-{\mathcal{O}})/2\,.\end{aligned}

(IV.171)

The identity operators along the two boundaries
\mathfrak{B}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}
can be identified respectively as

v_{e}=\frac{1}{2}(1+{\mathcal{O}})\,,\qquad v_{m}=\frac{1}{2}(1-{\mathcal{O}})\,.

(IV.172)

The linking actions of the
\mathsf{Ising}
symmetry generators are given by

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle:~1\to 1,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{e}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{e},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{m},

(IV.173)

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle:~1\to\sqrt{2},\qquad{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow-\sqrt{2}{\mathcal{O}},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{e}\rightarrow 0,\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\rightarrow 0\,.

This implies the following linking actions

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle:~v_{e}\rightarrow v_{e},\qquad\quad\qquad v_{m}\rightarrow v_{m}

(IV.174)

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle:~v_{e}\rightarrow\sqrt{2}v_{m},\qquad v_{m}\rightarrow\sqrt{2}v_{e}\,.

We can therefore identity the line operators on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
realizing the
\mathsf{Ising}
symmetry as

\displaystyle\phi(P)

\displaystyle=P_{ee}\oplus P_{mm}

(IV.175)

\displaystyle\phi(S)

\displaystyle=S_{em}\oplus S_{me}\,.

with the relative Euler term between
\mathfrak{B}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}
fixed such that the linking actions of boundary changing lines are

S_{em}:~v_{e}\to\sqrt{2}v_{m},\qquad S_{me}:~v_{m}\to\sqrt{2}v_{e}\,.

(IV.176)

The reader can check that these lines satisfy the
\mathsf{Ising}
fusion rules.

Mathematically, we have provided information about a pivotal tensor functor

\phi:~\mathsf{Ising}\to\mathsf{Ising}^{1}_{2\times 2}\,,

(IV.177)

where
\mathsf{Ising}^{e^{-\lambda}=1}_{2\times 2}
is the pivotal multi-fusion category formed by topological lines living on the underlying boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
.

IV.6
\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{N})
Symmetry

The Tambara-Yamagami
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
categories are a generalization of the Ising symmetry, denoted by

{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{N})\,.

(IV.178)

The simple lines are
A^{i}
and
S
with

A^{N}\cong 1\,,\quad A\otimes S\cong S\otimes A\cong S\,,\quad S\otimes S\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}A^{i}\,.

(IV.179)

The SymTFT
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{N}))
is constructed by gauging a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
outer automorphism of the 3d DW theory for
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
with anyons
L_{e,m}
such that
e,m\in\{0,\cdots,N-1\}
. The topological lines of the gauged theory, i.e. of the SymTFT of
\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{N})
, are

L_{e}^{\pm}\,,\qquad L_{e,m}\,,\qquad\Sigma_{e}^{\pm}\,.

(IV.180)

such that
e>m
for
L_{e,m}
. For more details on the fusions and S-matrices see
Kaidi et al. 2023a
;
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
. Let us focus on
N=4
for concreteness.
The Lagrangian algebras were determined in
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
to be

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}

\displaystyle=L_{0}^{+}+L_{0}^{-}+L_{1,0}+L_{2,0}+L_{3,0}

(IV.181)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}

\displaystyle=L_{0}^{+}+L_{0}^{-}+L_{2}^{+}+L_{2}^{-}+2L_{2,0}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}

\displaystyle=L_{0}^{+}+L_{2}^{-}+L_{2,0}+\Sigma_{1}^{+}+\Sigma_{3}^{-}\,.

The symmetry boundary is provided by

\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{4})}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\,.

(IV.182)

Solving the necessary conditions in appendix
B
we find the following

\begin{array}[]{c|c|l}\text{dim}&\mathcal{A}&\text{Algebra}\\
\hline\cr 4&{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}&L_{0}^{+}+L_{0}^{-}+L_{2,0}\\
4&{\mathcal{A}}_{2,3}&L_{0}^{+}+L_{2}^{-}+L_{2,0}\\
4&{\mathcal{A}}_{2}&L_{0}^{+}+L_{0}^{-}+L_{2}^{+}+L_{2}^{-}\\
4&{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{(2)}&L_{0}^{+}+L_{2}^{+}+L_{2,0}\\
2&{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}^{\prime}&L_{0}^{+}+L_{0}^{-}\\
2&{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{(3)}&L_{0}^{+}+L_{2}^{+}\\
2&{\mathcal{A}}_{2,3}^{\prime}&L_{0}^{+}+L_{2}^{-}\\
1&{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2,3}&L_{0}^{+}\\
\end{array}

(IV.183)

We indicate with the subscripts the Lagrangian algebras in which these algebras are contained in and with
dim
the dimension of the algebra (note these are all non-maximal since
\text{dim}<8
).

Not all of these correspond to actual condensable algebras, as we will see, as the conditions we solve are only necessary, not sufficient. In order to determine the condensable algebras among these, we check whether there is a consistent reduced topological order. This shows that
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{(2)}
for instance is not consistent, while
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{2,3}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}
are.

IV.6.1
Condensable Algebra
\mathcal{A}_{1,2}

Consider the interface characterized by the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}=L_{0}^{+}+L_{0}^{-}+L_{2,0}
. First we need to determine the reduced topological order
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
. Given the dimension of
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
equals 8 and the algebra is of dimension 4, the reduced topological order has dimension 2, and so it is either the toric code or the double-semion. We now show that it is the former

\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})=\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})\,.

(IV.184)

The Lagrangian algebra of
\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{4}))\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})}
that completes the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1,2}=

\displaystyle L_{0}^{+}+L_{0}^{-}+L_{2,0}+L_{2}^{+}\overline{e}+L_{2}^{-}\overline{e}+L_{2,0}\overline{e}

(IV.185)

\displaystyle+L_{1,0}\overline{m}+L_{3,0}\overline{m}+L_{2,1}\overline{f}+L_{3,2}\overline{f}\,.

From this, one finds the decomposable algebra associated to the underlying non-symmetric gapped boundary

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{L}_{e}\oplus 2\mathcal{L}_{m}\,,

(IV.186)

which implies that the underlying boundary of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
DW theory is reducible and given by

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}^{e}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{B}^{m}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{B}^{m}_{2}\,,

(IV.187)

where
\mathfrak{B}
’s are irreducible topological boundaries with
\mathfrak{B}_{e}^{0}
corresponding to the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{e}=1\oplus e
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}^{1}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{m}^{2}
corresponding to the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{e}=1\oplus m
. The boundary changing lines from
\mathfrak{B}_{i}^{m}
to
\mathfrak{B}_{j}^{m}
for
i\neq j
are the invertible lines
1_{ij},P_{ij}
, while the boundary changing lines between
\mathfrak{B}_{i}^{m}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{j}^{e}
are the non-invertible lines
S_{ij},S_{ji}
. The relative Euler terms between these boundaries are fixed later.

The club quiche and the resulting operators can be depicted as follows:

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{4}))
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}_{1}
{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}
L_{2,0}
L_{2,0}
L_{1,0}
L_{3,0}
L_{0}^{-}
m
e
m
=
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}_{e}\oplus 2\mathcal{L}_{m}
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}
{\mathcal{O}}_{e}
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}
{\mathcal{O}}_{-}
e
m
m

(IV.188)

The algebra of these operators can be derived to be

\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}_{-}{\mathcal{O}}_{-}&=1,\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{-}{\mathcal{E}}_{e}&=-{\mathcal{E}}_{e},\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{-}{\mathcal{O}}_{e}&={\mathcal{O}}_{e},\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{-}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}&={\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1},\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{-}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}&={\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3},\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{e}{\mathcal{E}}_{e}&=0,\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{e}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}&=\sqrt{2}{\mathcal{E}}^{3}_{m},\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{e}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}&=\sqrt{2}{\mathcal{E}}^{1}_{m},\\
\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}_{e}{\mathcal{O}}_{e}&=(1+{\mathcal{O}}_{-}),\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}{\mathcal{E}}_{e}&=(1-{\mathcal{O}}_{-}),\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}&=0,\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}&=0,\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}&=\sqrt{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{e},\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}&=\sqrt{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{e},\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}&=(1+{\mathcal{O}}_{-})\,.\end{aligned}

(IV.189)

Subsequently, it is again useful to define the combinations

\begin{aligned} v_{0}&=\frac{1}{2}(1-{\mathcal{O}}_{-})\,,\\
v_{1}&=\frac{1}{4}(1+{\mathcal{O}}_{-}+\sqrt{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{e})\,,\\
v_{2}&=\frac{1}{4}(1+{\mathcal{O}}_{-}-\sqrt{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{e})\,,\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} \tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{e}\,,\\
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}&=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}({\mathcal{E}}^{1}_{m}+{\mathcal{E}}^{3}_{m})\,,\\
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}&=\frac{i}{2\sqrt{2}}({\mathcal{E}}^{1}_{m}-{\mathcal{E}}^{3}_{m})\,,\\
\end{aligned}

(IV.190)

where one can also notice that

\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}=\frac{{\mathcal{E}}_{e}v_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}=\frac{{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}v_{1}}{\sqrt{2}}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}=-\frac{i{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}v_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\,.

(IV.191)

Here
v_{0}
is the identity local operator along the irreducible boundary
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{e}
while
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
are the identity local operators along the irreducible boundaries
\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{m}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{m}
respectively. Furthermore,
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}
is the end of
e
along
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{e}
and
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}
and
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}
are the ends of
m
along
\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{m}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{m}
respectively. This follows from the products

\displaystyle v_{i}v_{j}=\delta_{ij}v_{j}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{i}v_{j}=\delta_{ij}\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{j}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{i}\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{j}=\delta_{ij}v_{j}\,,

(IV.192)

for
i,j\in\{0,1,2\}
.

The linking actions of the
\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{4})
symmetry generators are given by

\displaystyle A^{k}

\displaystyle:\quad\begin{aligned} 1&\to 1,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}_{-}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{-},\qquad{\mathcal{O}}_{e}\rightarrow(-1)^{k}{\mathcal{O}}_{e},\qquad\\
{\mathcal{E}}_{e}&\rightarrow(-1)^{k}{\mathcal{E}}_{e},\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1}\rightarrow i^{k}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1},\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}\rightarrow(-i)^{k}{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3},\cr\end{aligned}

(IV.193)

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle:\quad 1\to 2,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}_{-}\rightarrow-2{\mathcal{O}}_{-},\qquad{\mathcal{O}}_{e},{\mathcal{E}}_{e},{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{3}\rightarrow 0\,,

which is also consistent with the fusion rules (
IV.189
). The symmetry action also implies the linking actions

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:\quad v_{0}\rightarrow v_{0}\,,\qquad v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{2}

(IV.194)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\rightarrow-\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\rightarrow\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}\rightarrow-\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle:\quad v_{0}\rightarrow 2(v_{1}+v_{2})\,,\qquad v_{1},v_{2}\rightarrow v_{0}

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0},\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1},\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}\rightarrow 0\,.

We can therefore identify the line operators on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
realizing the
\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})
symmetry as

\displaystyle\phi(A)

\displaystyle=P_{00}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus P_{21}

(IV.195)

\displaystyle\phi(S)

\displaystyle=S_{01}\oplus S_{02}\oplus S_{10}\oplus S_{20}\,,

where

S_{ij}\otimes S_{jk}=1_{ik}\oplus P_{ik}\,.

(IV.196)

One can then easily check that indeed

\phi(S)\otimes\phi(S)=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{3}\phi(A^{i})\,.

(IV.197)

The relative Euler terms are fixed as follows. Since the linking action of
A
on
v_{1}
produces
v_{2}
, the linking action of
1_{12}
on
v_{1}
must be

1_{12}:~v_{1}\to v_{2}\,,

(IV.198)

which means that there are no relative Euler terms between the magnetic boundaries
\mathfrak{B}^{m}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{m}_{2}
. On the other hand, to recover the linking action of
S
on
v_{0}
, we must have the following linking action of
S_{01}

S_{01}:~v_{0}\to 2v_{1}\,,

(IV.199)

which fixes the relative Euler term between electric and magnetic boundaries
\mathfrak{B}^{e}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{m}_{1}
.

Mathematically, this provides us with necessary information to describe a pivotal tensor functor

\phi:\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}(\mathfrak{B}^{\prime})\,,

(IV.200)

where
{\mathcal{S}}(\mathfrak{B}^{\prime})
is the pivotal multi-fusion category formed by the line operators living on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
also accounting for the above determined relative Euler terms.

IV.6.2
Condensable Algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{2,3}

For the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{2,3}
we find again that the reduced topological order is

\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})=\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})\,.

(IV.201)

The Lagrangian algebra of
\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{4}))\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})}
that completes
{\mathcal{A}}_{2,3}
in this case is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2,3}=

\displaystyle L_{0}^{+}+L_{2}^{-}+L_{2,0}+L_{2,0}\overline{e}+L_{2}^{+}\overline{e}+L_{0}^{-}\overline{e}

(IV.202)

\displaystyle+\Sigma_{1}^{+}\overline{m}+\Sigma_{1}^{-}\overline{f}+\Sigma_{3}^{+}\overline{f}+\Sigma_{3}^{-}\overline{m}\,.

From this, we see that the decomposable algebra associated to the underlying non-symmetric gapped boundary is

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}=2(1\oplus e)=2\mathcal{L}_{e}

(IV.203)

which corresponds to a reducible topological boundary condition of the toric code

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{e}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{e}\,.

(IV.204)

The topological line operators on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
are the same ones discussed in (
IV.110
), with fusions given by (
IV.111
) and (
IV.112
).

The operators we obtain from the club quiche are:

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{4}))
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
\mathcal{L}_{1}
{\mathcal{A}}_{2,3}
L_{2,0}
L_{2,0}
L_{0}^{-}
e
e
=
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
2\mathcal{L}_{e}
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
{\mathcal{O}}
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
e
e

(IV.205)

The products of these operators are determined to be

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{2}=1\,,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}={\mathcal{E}}_{2}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}={\mathcal{E}}_{1}

(IV.206)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{2}=1

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{2}=1\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{1}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}={\mathcal{O}}\,.

We can now define the combinations

\displaystyle v_{0}=(1+{\mathcal{O}})/2,\,\quad v_{1}=(1-{\mathcal{O}})/2,

(IV.207)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}=({\mathcal{E}}_{1}+{\mathcal{E}}_{2})/2,\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}=({\mathcal{E}}_{1}-{\mathcal{E}}_{2})/2\,,

where one can also notice that

\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}={\mathcal{E}}_{1}v_{0}={\mathcal{E}}_{2}v_{0}\,,\qquad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}={\mathcal{E}}_{1}v_{1}=-{\mathcal{E}}_{2}v_{1}\,.

(IV.208)

Here
v_{0}
and
v_{1}
are the identity local operators along the two irreducible boundaries
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{e}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{e}
respectively, while
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}
and
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}
are the end of
e
along the respective boundaries. This follows from the products

\displaystyle v_{i}v_{j}=\delta_{ij}v_{j},\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}^{2}=v_{0},\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{2}=v_{1},\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}=0,

(IV.209)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}v_{0}=\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0},\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}v_{1}=0,\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}v_{1}=\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1},\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}v_{0}=0\,.

The linking actions of the
\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})
symmetry generators are given by

\displaystyle A:

\displaystyle 1\rightarrow 1\,\quad{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{O}}\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

(IV.210)

\displaystyle S:

\displaystyle 1\rightarrow 2\,\quad{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow 0\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\rightarrow 0\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\rightarrow-2{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\,,

which implies the following linking actions

\displaystyle A:v_{0}\leftrightarrow v_{1}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\rightarrow-\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\rightarrow-\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}

(IV.211)

\displaystyle S:v_{0},v_{1}\rightarrow 1=v_{0}+v_{1}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\rightarrow\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}-\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\,,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\rightarrow\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}-\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\,.

We can therefore identify the line operators on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
realizing the
\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})
symmetry as

\displaystyle\phi(A)

\displaystyle=P_{01}\oplus P_{10}

(IV.212)

\displaystyle\phi(S)

\displaystyle=1_{01}\oplus P_{00}\oplus 1_{10}\oplus P_{11}\,.

One can easily check that these satisfy the
\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})
fusion rules, e.g.

[\phi(S)]^{2}=2\,1_{00}\oplus 2\,1_{11}\oplus 2\,P_{01}\oplus 2\,P_{10}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{3}\phi(A^{i})\,.

(IV.213)

From the linking actions one finds that there is no relative Euler term between
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{e}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{e}
.

Mathematically, this provides information about a pivotal tensor functor

\phi:\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})\rightarrow\text{Mat}_{2}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})

(IV.214)

where
\text{Mat}_{2}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
is the multi-fusion category formed by the topological line operators living on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
.

IV.6.3
Condensable algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}

In this case, the bulk phase
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}
is the double semion, with the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4}))\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}^{\omega})}
given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle=L_{0}^{+}\oplus L_{0}^{-}\oplus L_{2}^{+}\oplus L_{2}^{-}\oplus L_{1}^{+}s\oplus L_{1}^{-}s

(IV.215)

\displaystyle\oplus L_{3}^{+}s\oplus L_{3}^{-}s\oplus 2L_{3,1}\overline{s}\oplus 2L_{2,0}s\overline{s}\,.

From this, we see that the decomposable algebra associated to the underlying non-symmetric gapped boundary is

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}=2(1\oplus s\overline{s})=2\mathcal{L}_{s\overline{s}}\,,

(IV.216)

which corresponds to a reducible topological boundary condition of the double semion

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{s\overline{s}}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{s\overline{s}}\,.

(IV.217)

The topological line operators on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
are the same ones discussed in (
IV.110
), with fusions given by (
IV.111
) and (
IV.112
).

The operators we obtain from the club quiche are:

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4}))
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}^{\omega})
\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})}
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
L_{2,0}
L_{0}^{-}
s\overline{s}
=
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}^{\omega}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}})
2\mathcal{L}_{s\overline{s}}
{\mathcal{E}}_{1,2}
{\mathcal{O}}
s\overline{s}

(IV.218)

The products of these operators are determined to be

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{2}=1\,,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}={\mathcal{E}}_{1}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}=-{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

(IV.219)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{2}=(1+{\mathcal{O}})/2\,,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{2}=(1-{\mathcal{O}})/2\,,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{1}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}=0\,.

We can now define the combinations

\displaystyle v_{0}=(1+{\mathcal{O}})/2\,,

\displaystyle v_{1}=(1-{\mathcal{O}})/2

(IV.220)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}={\mathcal{E}}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}={\mathcal{E}}_{2}\,.

Here
v_{0}
and
v_{1}
are the identity local operators along the two irreducible boundaries
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{s\overline{s}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{s\overline{s}}
respectively, while
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}
and
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}
are the end of
s\overline{s}
along the respective boundaries. This follows from the products

\displaystyle v_{i}v_{j}=\delta_{ij}v_{j}\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}^{2}=v_{0}\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{2}=v_{1}\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}=0

(IV.221)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}v_{0}=\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}v_{1}=0\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}v_{1}=\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}v_{0}=0\,.

The linking actions of the
\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})
symmetry generators are given by

\displaystyle A:1\rightarrow 1\,\quad{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\rightarrow-{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

(IV.222)

\displaystyle S:1\rightarrow 2\,\quad{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow-2{\mathcal{O}}\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\rightarrow 0\,\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\rightarrow 0\,,

which implies the following linking actions

\displaystyle A:v_{0}\rightarrow v_{0}\,\quad v_{1}\rightarrow v_{1}\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\rightarrow-\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\rightarrow-\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}

(IV.223)

\displaystyle S:v_{0}\rightarrow 2v_{1}\,\quad v_{1}\rightarrow 2v_{0}\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}\rightarrow 0\,\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\rightarrow 0\,.

We can therefore identify the line operators on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
realizing the
\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})
symmetry as

\displaystyle\phi(A)

\displaystyle=P_{00}\oplus P_{11}

(IV.224)

\displaystyle\phi(S)

\displaystyle=1_{01}\oplus P_{01}\oplus 1_{10}\oplus P_{10}\,.

One can easily check that these satisfy the
\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})
fusion rules, e.g.

[\phi(S)]^{2}=2\,1_{00}\oplus 2\,1_{11}\oplus 2\,P_{00}\oplus 2\,P_{11}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{3}\phi(A^{i})\,.

(IV.225)

From the linking actions one finds that there is no relative Euler term between
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{s\overline{s}}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{1}^{s\overline{s}}
. Also note that the junction operators between the
\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{4})
line operators have to be chosen judiciously as in section
IV.2.3
, in order to be consistent with the ’t Hooft anomaly.

Mathematically, this provides information about a pivotal tensor functor

\phi:\mathsf{TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4})\rightarrow\text{Mat}_{2}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}^{\omega})\,,

(IV.226)

where
\text{Mat}_{2}(\mathsf{Vec}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}^{\omega})
is the multi-fusion category formed by the line operators living on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
.

V
Club Sandwich

Just like closing a quiche leads to a sandwich, closing a club quiche leads to a club sandwich. More precisely, a
d
-dimensional club sandwich
\mathfrak{S}^{C}_{d}
is obtained by supplying a
d
-dimensional club quiche (
IV.1
) on the right with a (possibly non-topological) boundary condition
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{d}
of the
(d+1)
-dimensional TQFT
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1}

\mathfrak{S}^{C}_{d}=(\mathfrak{B}_{d},\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1},{\mathcal{I}}_{d},\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1},\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{d})\,.

(V.1)

Applied to
\mathfrak{Z}=\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
, i.e. in the context of the SymTFT, the club sandwich becomes

\mathfrak{S}^{C}_{d}=(\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}),{\mathcal{I}}_{d},\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1},\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{d})\,,

(V.2)

which we depict as

\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathcal{S}}
{\mathcal{I}}_{d}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{d}
=
\mathfrak{T}_{d}
{\mathcal{S}}

(V.3)

The full interval compactification shown on the right hand side involves collapsing both intervals occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}})
and
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1}
, respectively. The result is an
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric QFT
\mathfrak{T}_{d}
. Thus, a club quiche
\mathcal{Q}^{C}_{d}
can be viewed as a machine mapping (possibly non-topological) boundary conditions of a
(d+1)
-dimensional TQFT
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1}
to
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric
d
-dimensional QFTs

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{C}_{d}:

\displaystyle\{\text{Boundaries of TQFT }\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1}\}

(V.4)

\displaystyle\to\,\{{\mathcal{S}}\text{
-symmetric QFTs}\}\,.

The standard SymTFT sandwich in this context is obtained by specializing to
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1}
to be the trivial topological order.

V.1
Kennedy-Tasaki (KT) Transformations

The club sandwich realizes what are known as the Kennedy-Tasaki (KT) transformations and generalizations thereof.
For this, specialize
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1}
to be the SymTFT associated to a symmetry
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}

\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{d+1}=\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})\,.

(V.5)

Then the club sandwich construction provides a map

{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathcal{I}}_{d}}^{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}:~\text{\lx@text@lbrace${\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}$-symmetric QFTs\lx@text@rbrace$\,\to\,$\lx@text@lbrace${\mathcal{S}}$-symmetric QFTs\lx@text@rbrace}

(V.6)

We begin with an
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric
d
-dimensional QFT
\mathfrak{T}_{d}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, which by the sandwich construction provides a generally non-topological boundary condition
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}_{d}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}}
of the SymTFT
\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}

\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{d}}
=
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{d}

(V.7)

Inserting this boundary on the right hand side of a club sandwich, results upon interval compactification in an
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric QFT
\mathfrak{T}_{d}^{\mathcal{S}}

\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathcal{S}}
{\mathcal{I}}_{d}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{d}}
=
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{d}

(V.8)

The map (
V.6
) is then

{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}:~\mathfrak{T}_{d}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathfrak{T}_{d}^{\mathcal{S}}

(V.9)

We refer to such a transformation as a
KT transformation
(short for Kennedy-Tasaki). The physical
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{d}}
associated to the
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{d}
appearing in its sandwich construction

\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{d}}
=
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{d}

(V.10)

is obtained simply by compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
in the club sandwich (
V.8
) leading to the composition of
{\mathcal{I}}_{d}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}_{d}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}}
,
i.e. we have

\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathcal{S}}
{\mathcal{I}}_{d}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}_{d}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}}
=
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathcal{S}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}_{d}^{\mathcal{S}}}

(V.11)

Let us restrict to minimal club quiche
\mathcal{Q}^{C}_{d}
. Then, the above map
{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
respects irreducibility:

{Irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric
d
-dimensional QFTs}
{Irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric
d
-dimensional QFTs}
{\mathcal{K}}^{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}
:

(V.12)

In order to see this, notice that irreducibility means that the physical boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{d}}
carries no topological local operators that are not proportional to identity, or more succinctly no non-identity topological local operators. Minimality of the club quiche means that no topological bulk line of
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
can end along the topological interface
{\mathcal{I}}_{d}
. Consequently, no new topological local operators are produced by compactification of bulk lines in the compactification shown in (
V.11
). Thus, the physical boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{d}}
carries no non-identity topological local operators and hence
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{d}
is irreducible. We refer to KT transformations arising from minimal club quiches as
minimal KT transformations
. Note that, although the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{d}
is irreducible as an
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric QFT, it may be reducible when we forget the
{\mathcal{S}}
symmetry, which happens whenever the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}_{d}
of
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
produced by compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}})

\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathcal{S}}
{\mathcal{I}}_{d}
=
\mathfrak{Z}_{d+1}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}

(V.13)

is reducible.

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a 2d TQFT lying in an irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric (1+1)d gapped phase described by a Lagrangian algebra

\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}}=\bigoplus_{a^{\prime}}n_{a^{\prime}}^{\text{phys}}(\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}})^{*}\,.

(V.14)

Applying a minimal KT transformation
{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
we find a 2d TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
lying in an irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric (1+1)d gapped phase described by the Lagrangian algebra

\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}}=\bigoplus_{a}n_{a,a^{\prime}}n_{a^{\prime}}^{\text{phys}}\mathbf{Q}_{a}\,.

(V.15)

Computational realization.

At the level of generalized charges, a minimal 2d KT transformation works as follows. A property of a minimal Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}})\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
is that given a simple anyon
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\in\overline{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
, there always exists at least one term in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
of the form

n_{aa^{\prime}}\mathbf{Q}_{a}\overline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}

(V.16)

for some simple anyon
\mathbf{Q}_{a}\in\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
and
n_{aa^{\prime}}>0
. This means that a simple anyon
\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
can always be converted into some anyon of
\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
as it passes through the interface
{\mathcal{I}}
from the right to the left. That is, a minimal interface
{\mathcal{I}}
converts each irreducible generalized charge of
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
into a (possibly reducible) generalized charge of
{\mathcal{S}}
. This map between generalized charges is mathematically encoded in a functor

F_{\mathcal{I}}:~\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}})\,,

(V.17)

which takes the form

F_{\mathcal{I}}(\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}})=\bigoplus_{a}n_{aa^{\prime}}\mathbf{Q}^{*}_{a}\,,

(V.18)

where the coefficients
n_{aa^{\prime}}
appear in the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
as in (
IV.9
).

Let’s discuss the KT transformations induced by the minimal club quiches studied in the previous section in turn.

V.2
KT from
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
Symmetry I

First consider the club quiche (

) associated to the algebra

, which was studied in section

.

The symmetry before the KT transformation is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}

(V.19)

and the symmetry obtained after the KT transformation is
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{4}}\,.

(V.20)

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric 2d QFT. We consider the Club Sandwich:

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{4}})
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{4}}}
{\mathcal{A}}_{e}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=2\mathcal{L}_{e}

{\mathcal{E}}_{+}

{\mathcal{E}}_{-}
e^{\prime}

({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{\prime}})_{\partial}

{\mathcal{O}}_{e}

(V.21)

We will discuss the various operators shortly.

The KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric 2d QFT, whose underlying non-symmetric QFT is easily obtained by noting from (
IV.46
) that the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
obtained by compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\oplus\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\,.

(V.22)

Thus, the QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
involves two copies of the sandwich construction for the QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, and hence we can write

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{0}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{1}\,,

(V.23)

where
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{i}
is a copy of the QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. One may say that
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
is a QFT with two
universes
, such that each universe comprises of a copy of QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. Note that the relative Euler term between the two
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
universes is trivial, which is a consequence of the fact that the relative Euler term between the two irreducible boundaries comprising
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is trivial, as displayed in (
IV.46
).

Under the full club sandwich compactification, the lines of the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
descend to lines of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
.
The line operators living on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
are labeled as in (
IV.36
). The line
1_{ii}
descends to the identity line on
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{i}
,
and the line
P_{ii}
descends to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{i}
. The lines
1_{01}
and
1_{10}
descend to lines exchanging the two copies of the QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. These two lines
1_{01}
and
1_{10}
have trivial quantum dimensions, correlated to the fact that there is no relative Euler term between the two
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
universes. Finally the lines
P_{10}
and
P_{01}
are combinations shown in (
IV.38
).

From (
IV.49
), we learn that the generator
P
of the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry
{\mathcal{S}}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
is realized by the line operator

\phi(P)=1_{01}\oplus P_{10}\,.

(V.24)

Note that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
is identified as the diagonal
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
of the two
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetries acting on the individual
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
universes

\phi(P^{2})=P_{00}\oplus P_{11}\,.

(V.25)

That is, the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry exchanges the two universes, but the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
is completely localized within individual universes. We may thus express the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
as

(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{0}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{1}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}\quad=
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

(V.26)

From the expression for the Lagrangian algebra

in (

) we observe that the map

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})\to\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{4}})

\begin{aligned} 1&\to 1\oplus e^{2},\\

V.3
KT from
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
Symmetry II

Now consider the club quiche (
IV.61
) associated to the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{m}=1\oplus m^{2}
, which was studied in section
IV.2.2
.

As in the previous example, the symmetry before the KT transformation is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}

(V.33)

and the symmetry obtained after the KT transformation is
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{4}}\,.

(V.34)

Again let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric 2d QFT, and we want to understand its KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
, which is a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric 2d QFT.

The boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
obtained by compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
is

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}

(V.35)

and hence the QFT underlying
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
is exactly the same as the QFT underlying
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. From (
IV.63
), we know that the generator
P
of the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry is realized as the generator
P^{\prime}
of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry, and hence we can express
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

(V.36)

In other words, we have used the non-trivial homomorphism
\mathbb{Z}_{4}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to regard a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric QFT as a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric QFT.

From the expression (
IV.57
) for
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
we observe that the map

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})\to\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{4}})

(V.37)

of generalized charges is

\begin{aligned} 1&\to 1\oplus m^{2},\\
e^{\prime}&\to e^{2}\oplus e^{2}m^{2},\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} m^{\prime}&\to m\oplus m^{3}\\
e^{\prime}m^{\prime}&\to e^{2}m\oplus e^{2}m^{3}\end{aligned}

(V.38)

According to this map the operators are identified as follows:

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to two operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
: one of them being an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}
that is uncharged under

Now consider the club quiche (

) associated to the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{em}
, which was studied in section

.

The symmetry before the KT transformation is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
with non-trivial ’t Hooft anomaly

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Vec}^{\omega}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}

(V.43)

and the symmetry obtained after the KT transformation is a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{4}}\,.

(V.44)

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a 2d QFT with anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetry. We want to understand its KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
which is a 2d QFT with non-anomalous

-symmetry. For this purpose, first note that the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
obtained by compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
is

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\,.

(V.45)

and hence the QFT underlying
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
is exactly the same as the QFT underlying
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. From (
IV.75
), we know that the generator
P
of the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry is realized as the generator
P^{\prime}
of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry, and hence we can express

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

(V.46)

Even though the map at the level of topological lines is simply a homomorphism
\mathbb{Z}_{4}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(1,P,P^{2},P^{3})\to(1,P^{\prime},1,P^{\prime})

(V.47)

the junctions of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry lines are chosen in a fashion that does not follow from the above homomorphism. See section
IV.2.3
for a detailed discussion.

From the expression (

) for

V.5
KT from
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
to
S_{3}
Symmetry

Consider the club quiche (
IV.98
) associated to the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{1_{-}}
, which was studied in section
IV.3.1
.

The symmetry before the KT transformation maps a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
to an
S_{3}
symmetry

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}\to{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}}\,.

(V.57)

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric 2d QFT. Its KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
is an
S_{3}
-symmetric 2d QFT.

The boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
obtained by compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
is

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\oplus\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\,.

(V.58)

Thus, the QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
involves two copies of the sandwich construction for the QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, and hence we can write

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{0}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{1}\,,

(V.59)

where
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{i}
is a copy of the QFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. Note that the relative Euler term between the two
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
universes is trivial, which is a consequence of the fact that the relative Euler term between the two irreducible boundaries comprising
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is trivial, as displayed in (
IV.104
).

The lines on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
descend to lines on
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
, which we label in the same way.
1_{ii}
is the identity of
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{i}
,
P_{ii}
is the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry of
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{i}
and
P^{2}_{ii}
is its square. The rest of the lines are universe changing. Equation (
IV.103
) describes how the
S_{3}
symmetry is then realized on
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
, which we schematically depict as

(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{0}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{1}
\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{-1}
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}\quad=
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(V.60)

displaying the actions of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroups of
S_{3}
generated respectively by
a
and
b
, and the notation
\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{-1}
captures the fact that the generator
\phi(a)
of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
inside
S_{3}
when acting on the universe
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{1}
is the inverse
P_{11}^{2}
of the generator
P_{11}
of the original
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry of
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{1}
.

From the expression for the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
IV.90
) we observe that the map

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}})\to\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}})

(V.61)

m^{2}&\to a_{1},\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} e&\to E,\\

em^{2}&\to a_{\omega^{2}},\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} e^{2}&\to E\\

e^{2}m^{2}&\to a_{\omega}\,.\end{aligned}

on these operators is

b:~{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{b}\to(-1)^{k}{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{b},\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{ab}\leftrightarrow(-1)^{k}{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a^{2}b}\,,

(V.75)

We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a^{i}b}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}m,i}\,,

(V.76)

where
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}m,i}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}m}
in the universe
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})_{i}
.

V.7
KT from
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to
S_{3}
Symmetry II

Consider the club quiche (
IV.127
) associated to the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}
, which was studied in section
IV.3.3
. The associated KT transformation converts
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry into
S_{3}
symmetry

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\quad\longrightarrow\quad{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}}\,.

(V.77)

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric 2d QFT. Its KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
is an
S_{3}
-symmetric 2d QFT which can be expressed as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
S_{3}

(V.78)

i.e. we regard the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
as an
S_{3}
-symmetric theory simply by pulling back symmetries using the non-trivial homomorphism
S_{3}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

From the expression for the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
IV.123
) we observe that the map

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})\to\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{S_{3}})

(V.79)

of generalized charges is

1\to 1\oplus a_{1},\quad e\to 1_{-}\oplus a_{1},\quad m\to b_{+},\quad em\to b_{-}\,.

(V.80)

According to this map:

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to three operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
: one of them being an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}
that is uncharged under
S_{3}
, and the other two
{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a},{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{2}}
being in
a,a^{2}
-twisted sectors which are uncharged under
a
but exchanged by
b
. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}\,,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{k}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}\,,

(V.81)

where
{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{k}}
is the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
viewed as lying in the
a^{k}
-twisted sector.

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
charged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to three operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
: one of them being an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
in representation
1_{-}
of
S_{3}
, and the other two
{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a},{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a^{2}}
being in
a,a^{2}
-twisted sectors which are uncharged under
a
but exchanged by
b
. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}\,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a^{k}}=(-1)^{k}{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}\,,

(V.82)

where
{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a^{k}}
is the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}
viewed as lying in the
a^{k}
-twisted sector.

•

An operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}m}
in
P
-twisted sector of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, where
P
is the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry before KT transformation, and having charge
k
under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to three operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
:
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a^{i}b}
in
a^{i}b
-twisted sector. The action of
b
on these operators is described in (
V.75
). We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a^{i}b}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}m}\,,

(V.83)

where
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a^{i}b}
is the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}m}
viewed as lying in the
a^{i}b
-twisted sector.

V.8
KT from
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
to
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
Symmetry

Consider the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
club quiche (

) associated to the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{1_{-}}
studied in section

. The associated KT transformation converts
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry into
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}\quad\longrightarrow\quad{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})\,.

(V.84)

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric 2d QFT. Its KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
is a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric 2d QFT which can be expressed as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})

(V.85)

i.e. the underlying 2d QFT for
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
is the same as for
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}

).

From the expression for the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
IV.142
) we observe that the map

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}})\to\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3}))

(V.86)

of generalized charges is

\begin{aligned} 1&\to 1\oplus 1_{-},\\
e&\to a_{1},\\
e^{2}&\to a_{1},\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} m&\to E,\\
em&\to a_{\omega},\\
e^{2}m&\to a_{\omega^{2}},\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} m^{2}&\to E\\
em^{2}&\to a_{\omega^{2}}\\
e^{2}m^{2}&\to a_{\omega}\end{aligned}

(V.87)

According to this map:

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
descends to two operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
: one of them being an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}
that is uncharged under
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
, and the other being an operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
in the
1_{-}
-twisted sector that is uncharged under
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
. We call such an operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
as an operator living in
1_{-}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}\,,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}\,,

(V.88)

where
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
is the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
viewed as lying in the
1_{-}
-twisted sector.

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
having charge
k=1,2
under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
descends to two operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
: one of them being an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{1},u}
and the other operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{1},t}
in
1_{-}
-twisted sector. These operators are uncharged under
1_{-}
, but mixed together by the action of
E
as described in section 5.2.2 of
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
. These mixings imply that the linking action of
E
on these operators is
-1
. We say that the two operators lie in an
a_{1}
-multiplet. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{1},u}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}}\,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{1},t}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}}\,,

(V.89)

where
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{1},t}
is the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{k}}
viewed as lying in the
1_{-}
-twisted sector. The linking action of
E=P\oplus P^{2}
is
-1
because
\omega+\omega^{2}=-1
.

•

An operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{m^{k}}
, for
k=1,2
, in
P^{k}
-twisted sector of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, where
P
is the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry before KT transformation, and uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
descends to two operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
:
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,+}
in
E
-twisted sector and
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,-}
sitting between the lines
E
and
1_{-}
. We say that the two operators lie in an
E
-multiplet. These operators are uncharged under
1_{-}
, but mixed together by the action of
E
as described in section 5.2.3 of
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,+}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{m^{k}}\,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,-}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{m^{k}}\,,

(V.90)

where
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,\pm}
is the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{m^{k}}
viewed as lying at the end of
E=P\oplus P^{2}
line, since it lies at the end of one of these lines, and the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,-}
is additionally regarded as attached also the
1_{-}
line.

•

An operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{i}m^{k}}
, for
i,k\in\{1,2\}
, in
P^{k}
-twisted sector of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
and having charge
i
under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
descends to two operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
:
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{\omega^{ki}},+}
in
E
-twisted sector and
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{\omega^{ki}},-}
sitting between the lines
E
and
1_{-}
. These operators are uncharged under
1_{-}
, but mixed together by the action of
E
. We say that the two operators lie in an
a_{\omega^{ki}}
-multiplet. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{\omega^{ki}},+}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{i}m^{k}}\,\qquad{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{\omega^{ki}},+}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e^{i}m^{k}}\,.

(V.91)

V.9
KT from
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
Symmetry I

Consider the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
club quiche (

) associated to the algebra

studied in section

. The associated KT transformation converts a

symmetry

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\quad\longrightarrow\quad{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})\,.

(V.92)

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric 2d QFT. Its KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
is a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric 2d QFT which can be expressed as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})

(V.93)

i.e. the underlying 2d QFT for
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
is the same as for
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, with the

).

From the expression for the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
IV.135
) we observe that the map

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})\to\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3}))

(V.94)

of generalized charges is

1\to 1\oplus E,\quad m\to 1_{-}\oplus E,\quad e\to b_{+},\quad em\to b_{-}\,.

(V.95)

According to this map:

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
: one of them being an untwisted operator

, and the other two being operators

lying in an

-multiplet of

{\mathcal{O}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,+}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,-}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}\otimes\text{id}_{P}\,,

where

is the operator

viewed as lying at the end of

line, since the identity line is a part of

, and the operator

is obtained by fusing

with the identity operator
\text{id}_{P}
living along the topological line
P
generating the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry before KT transformation. This operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}\otimes\text{id}_{P}
can then be regarded as transitioning between
E
and
1_{-}
as both these lines involve the line
P
.

•

A
P
-twisted sector operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{m}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to three operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
:
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
living in
1_{-}
-multiplet, and
{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,+},{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,-}
in
E
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{m}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,+}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{m}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,-}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{m}\,.

(V.97)

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
charged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to three operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
:
{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+}}
which is untwisted,
{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},+}
lying in
E
-twisted sector, and
{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},-}
lying between
E
and
1_{-}
lines. The three operators are charged under
1_{-}
and mixed together by

as described in section 5.2.4 of
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
. We say that the three operators lie in a
b_{+}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},+}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},-}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}\otimes\text{id}_{P}\,.

(V.98)

•

A
P
-twisted sector operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{em}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
charged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to three operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
:
{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{-}}
lying in
1_{-}
-twisted sector,
{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{-},+}
lying in
E
-twisted sector, and
{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{-},-}
lying between
E
and
1_{-}
lines. The three operators are charged under
1_{-}
and mixed together by

. We say that the three operators lie in a

-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. We can recognize these operators as

{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{-}}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{em}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{-},+}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{em}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{-},-}={\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{em}\,.

(V.99)

V.10
KT from
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
Symmetry II

Consider the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
club quiche (
IV.154
) associated to the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{a_{1}}
studied in section
IV.4.3
. The associated KT transformation converts a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry into a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\quad\longrightarrow\quad{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})\,.

(V.100)

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric 2d QFT. Let’s understand its KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
which is a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric 2d QFT.

The boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
obtained after compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
is

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{\sqrt{2}}

(V.101)

as in (
IV.150
), where
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is the boundary of
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
obtained by gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
and the subscript
\sqrt{2}
describes that we additionally stack
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
with a non-trivial Euler term. The 2d QFT underlying
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
is thus

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{\sqrt{2}}\,,

(V.102)

where
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{\sqrt{2}}
is obtained by gauging the
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
and stacking an Euler term. For further proceedings, let us define

\mathfrak{T}_{e}:=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\,,\qquad\mathfrak{T}_{m}:=(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{\sqrt{2}}\,.

(V.103)

In other words,
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
comprises of two universes described respectively by theories
\mathfrak{T}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{T}_{m}
.

The lines living on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
are described in (
IV.151
). These lines descend to line operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
as follows. The line
1_{ii}
becomes the identity line in
\mathfrak{T}_{i}
, while the line
P_{ii}
becomes the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry generator of
\mathfrak{T}_{i}
. Here
P_{ee}
is the generator of the original
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, while
P_{mm}
is the generator of the dual
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
obtained after gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. The lines
S_{em}
and
S_{me}
are universe changing lines. Their linking actions on identity local operators
\text{id}_{e}
and
\text{id}_{m}
of
\mathfrak{T}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{T}_{m}
respectively are

S_{em}:~\text{id}_{e}\to 2\text{id}_{m}\,\qquad S_{me}:~\text{id}_{m}\to\text{id}_{e}\,,

(V.104)

which follows from (
IV.160
).

The
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry generators are identified in terms of these lines as in (
IV.159
), and thus we can schematically depict the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
as

\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{\sqrt{2}}
1_{-}
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}\quad=
E
E

(V.105)

Here
E
acts as
P_{ee}
as well as between the two summands as described in (
IV.159
).
From the expression for the Lagrangian algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
IV.123
) we observe that the map

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})\to\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3}))

(V.106)

of generalized charges is

1\to 1\oplus a_{1},\quad e\to 1_{-}\oplus a_{1},\quad m\to b_{+},\quad em\to b_{-}\,.

(V.107)

According to this map:

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to three operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
: one of them being an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
that is uncharged under
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
, and the other two being operators
{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},u},{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},t}
lying in an
a_{1}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry discussed above. We can recognize these operators as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{1}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{e}+({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{m}

(V.108)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},u}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{e}-2({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{m}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{e}}{1+2\omega}\,,

where
\omega
is a third root of unity.
Here
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{e}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{T}_{e}
and
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{m}
is the operator in
\mathfrak{T}_{m}
obtained after gauging
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Both
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{e}
and
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})_{m}
are uncharged under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetries of
\mathfrak{T}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{T}_{m}
respectively. The form of
{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},u}
follows from the identification of operator
{\mathcal{O}}
in terms of
v_{e}
and
v_{m}
in (
IV.156
) since we have that
{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},u}
is the fusion
{\mathcal{O}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
. The form of the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},t}
results from a particular choice of junction local operators between
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
lines and was derived in eqn. (5.94) of
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
. Note that the operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},t}
can be regarded as living in the
1_{-}
-twisted sector because
1_{-}
contains the identity line
1_{ee}
for
\mathfrak{T}_{e}
.

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
charged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to three operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
: one of them
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
living in
1_{-}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
, and the other two being operators
{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},u},{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},t}
lying in an
a_{1}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry discussed above. We can recognize these operators as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e})_{e}+({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e})_{m}

(V.109)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},u}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e})_{e}}{1+2\omega}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},t}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e})_{e}-2({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e})_{m}\,.

Here
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e})_{e}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e}
in
\mathfrak{T}_{e}
and
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e})_{m}
is the operator in
\mathfrak{T}_{m}
obtained after gauging
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which is thus in
P_{mm}
twisted sector and uncharged under
P_{mm}
. These operators are uncharged under
1_{-}
as it involves only
P_{mm}
but
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e})_{m}
is uncharged under
P_{mm}
. Note that the twisted and untwisted components of the
a_{1}
-multiplet
\{{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},u},{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},t}\}
are “reversed” as compared to the twisted and untwisted components of the
a_{1}
-multiplet
\{{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},u},{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a_{1},t}\}
described in (
V.108
). This is because we now have
{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},t}={\mathcal{O}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}

V.11

\mathsf{Ising}

Consider the
\mathsf{Ising}

(V.112)

Let
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric 2d QFT. Let’s understand its KT transformation
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
which is an
\mathsf{Ising}
-symmetric 2d QFT.

The boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
obtained after compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
is

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})

as in (
IV.169
), where
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

obtained by gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. The 2d QFT underlying

is thus

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})\,,

(V.114)

\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}

\mathfrak{T}_{e}:=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\,,\qquad\mathfrak{T}_{m}:=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,.

(V.115)

In other words,
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
comprises of two universes described respectively by theories
\mathfrak{T}_{e}
and
\mathfrak{T}_{m}
.

The lines living on

and their map to lines of

. However, the linking actions on identity local operators

and

and
\mathfrak{T}_{m}
respectively of the universe changing lines are now

S_{em}:~\text{id}_{e}\to\sqrt{2}\text{id}_{m}\,\qquad S_{me}:~\text{id}_{m}\to\sqrt{2}\text{id}_{e}\,,

(V.116)

which follows from (
IV.176
).

The
\mathsf{Ising}
symmetry generators are identified in terms of these lines as in (

), and thus we can schematically depict the

-symmetric theory
