\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})

(V.127)

where we remind the reader that

The action of the symmetry

) and we can schematically depict

with

S
A
A

(V.128)

we

\displaystyle 1

(V.130)

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle\to L_{1,0}+L_{3,0}

\displaystyle em

\displaystyle\to L_{2,1}+L_{3,2}\,.

These imply the following maps on operators:

•

of

To conclude, the action of the

\displaystyle A^{k}:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}_{0}^{-}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{0}^{-}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}^{u,(1)}\to(-1)^{k}{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}^{u,(1)}

(V.131)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}^{t,(1)}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}^{t,(1)}

\displaystyle S:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}_{0}^{-}\to-{\mathcal{O}}_{0}^{-}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}^{u,(1)}\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}^{t,(1)}\,.

This is simply the action upon moving past the lines, which is in general different from the linking action of lines on these operators.

We can recognize these operators as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{e}_{0}+({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{m}_{1}+({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{m}_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}^{t,(1)}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})^{e}_{0}\,,

where

VI
Phase Transitions: New from Old

One of the key applications of the club sandwich constructions, i.e. the KT transformations, is the study of phase transitions between gapped phases with categorical symmetries. This comprises a central aspect of the categorical Landau paradigm that was developed in
Bhardwaj et al. 2024a
;
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
. In there the main result was the characterization of gapped phases with categorical symmetries and their associated order parameters using the SymTFT.

For group-like symmetries this was discussed in
Chatterjee et al. 2022
using a similar SymTFT description. Here we extend this to include non-invertible symmetries such as
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
and
\mathsf{TY}(\mathbb{Z}_{N})
.

VI.1
General Setup

Suppose that we know an irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric
d
-dimensional CFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}
(note that unlike the earlier parts of the paper, the subscripts will not refer to spacetime dimensions anymore) admitting a relevant operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
that is uncharged under
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
(sometimes also referred to as an
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric local operator), such that deforming the CFT with
+{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
leads to an irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric
d
-dimensional TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
, and deforming the CFT with
-{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
leads to another irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric
d
-dimensional TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}

\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}
-{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
+{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}

(VI.1)

In such a situation,
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}
is referred to as a
phase transition
between the
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric gapped phases
[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}]
and
[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}]
.

By applying a minimal KT transformation
{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, we obtain an irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric
d
-dimensional CFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
acting as a phase transition between two irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric gapped phases
\left[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\right]
and
\left[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\right]
:

\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{C}
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}
-{\mathcal{O}}
+{\mathcal{O}}

(VI.2)

Here
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
is obtained from
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{i}
, for
i\in\{1,2,C\}
, by applying the KT transformation
{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{I}}^{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
. In order to see this, note that since
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
is uncharged, it arises in the
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-sandwich construction from an operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{\partial}
completely localized along the physical boundary:
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}}

\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}}
=
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{\partial}
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}

(VI.3)

Deforming the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}}
by
\pm{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{\partial}
leads to topological physical boundaries
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1,2}}
for
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1,2}

\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}}
-{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{\partial}
+{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{\partial}

(VI.4)

since the sandwich construction with these physical boundaries describes the phase transition (
VI.1
).
This can be thought of as a boundary phase transition. We can now feed in this boundary phase transition into a club sandwich to obtain an
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric phase transition. First compactifying the interval occupied by
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
, we obtain an operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{\partial}
localized along
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{C}}

\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}}
{\mathcal{I}}
=
\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{C}}
{\mathcal{O}}_{\partial}
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{\partial}

(VI.5)

that generates a boundary phase transition

\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{C}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}
-{\mathcal{O}}_{\partial}
+{\mathcal{O}}_{\partial}

(VI.6)

After the full club sandwich compactification,
{\mathcal{O}}_{\partial}
descends to an operator
{\mathcal{O}}
in
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
uncharged under the symmetry
{\mathcal{S}}

\mathfrak{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{C}}
=
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{C}
{\mathcal{O}}_{\partial}
{\mathcal{O}}

(VI.7)

which is responsible for the desired
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric phase transition
(
VI.2
).

In conclusion, one can obtain new phase transitions from known phase transitions by applying minimal KT transformations. This is quite useful as a minimal KT transformation maps
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric theories to
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric theories where
{\mathcal{S}}
is morally larger than
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}

``\,{\mathcal{S}}>{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}\,\text{''}\,.

(VI.8)

One can then begin with a small enough
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
like
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
for which a transition is well-known, and iteratively keep applying KT transformations to generate new phase transitions for larger and larger symmetries, which may not be invertible in general.

VI.2
Input Phase Transitions

Let us now discuss a couple of known phase transitions that we will use to construct new phase transitions by applying KT transformations to them.

VI.2.1
The Critical Ising Model

The first one is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric transition provided by the 2d Ising CFT. The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry is the spin flip symmetry that we label as
\eta
. We will focus on three special local operators in the Ising CFT, namely the order/spin operator
\sigma
, the disorder operator
\mu
, and the energy operator
\epsilon
. The order operator
\sigma
is an untwisted sector local operator (i.e. a local operator unattached to any line operators) that is charged non-trivially under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry
\eta

\eta:~\sigma\to-\sigma

(VI.9)

On the other hand, the disorder operator
\mu
is an
\eta
-twisted sector local operator, i.e. it is attached to the line
\eta
generating the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry. Additionally it carries trivial
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
charge

\eta:~\mu\to\mu

(VI.10)

Finally, the energy operator
\epsilon
is also an untwisted operator, but is uncharged under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry

\eta:~\epsilon\to\epsilon

(VI.11)

These three operators correspond to three different generalized charges for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry. As we discussed in section
III.1
, the generalized charges of a symmetry are captured by the anyons of its associated SymTFT. In the case of non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry, the SymTFT
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})
is the toric code, whose anyons are labeled as in (
IV.106
). Furthermore, the symmetry boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
is taken to be the one corresponding to the Lagrangian algebra

\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}=1\oplus e

(VI.12)

Then the generalized charges for the above three operators are

q(\sigma)=e,\qquad q(\mu)=m,\qquad q(\epsilon)=1

(VI.13)

The sandwich construction of an operator involves compactification of the bulk line capturing its generalized charge. In the current case, the sandwich construction of the three operators is

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\text{Ising}}
=
Ising
e
\sigma
\sigma_{\partial}
\mu_{\partial}
\mu
m
\epsilon_{\partial}
\epsilon

(VI.14)

in terms of local operators
\sigma_{\partial}
,
\mu_{\partial}
and
\epsilon_{\partial}
on the physical boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\text{Ising}}
. Note that even though the
e
line completely ends along the symmetry boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
, the
m
line does not end but becomes the line
P
living on
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
, which is suppressed in the above figure. The boundary line
P
becomes the line
\eta
after the interval compactification, as a consequence of which after the compactification the local operator
\mu
is attached to the
\eta
line. The charges under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
arise from the fact that the end of line
e
along
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
is charged under the boundary line
P
, while the end of line
m
along
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
is uncharged under the boundary line
P
.

Deforming the Ising CFT by the energy operator
\epsilon
leads to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric gapped phases in the IR, which are different depending on the sign of the deformation. The two gapped phases are

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ SSB phase for $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ symmetry}

(VI.15)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{Trivial gapped phase for $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ symmetry}

Upto an overall Euler term, the TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}
comprises of two universes, with both universes occupied by the trivial theory

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}=\text{Trivial}_{0}\,\oplus\,\text{Trivial}_{1}

(VI.16)

where the two copies of the trivial theory are distinguished by subscript labels
0,1
. The identity local operators
v_{0},v_{1}
of the two trivial theories satisfy product rules

v_{0}^{2}=v_{0},\qquad v_{1}^{2}=v_{1},\qquad v_{0}v_{1}=0

(VI.17)

and are also referred to as vacua. The topological line operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}
are

1_{ij},\qquad i,j\in\{0,1\}

(VI.18)

where
1_{ii}
is the identity line in
\text{Trivial}_{i}
, and
1_{01},1_{10}
exchange the two copies of trivial theory. There is no relative Euler term, and hence the linking actions of
1_{01},1_{10}
on the vacua are

1_{01}:~v_{0}\to v_{1},\qquad 1_{10}:~v_{1}\to v_{0}

(VI.19)

The fusion rules of the lines are

1_{ij}\otimes 1_{jk}=1_{ik}\,.

(VI.20)

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry
\eta
is identified in the IR as

\eta\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{10}\,,

(VI.21)

which exchanges the two vacua

\eta:~v_{0}\leftrightarrow v_{1}\,.

(VI.22)

As a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric theory, we may represent
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}
as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}=\text{Trivial}_{0}\,\oplus\,\text{Trivial}_{1}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(VI.23)

The spin operator
\sigma
of the Ising CFT acquires a non-zero vacuum expectation value (vev) in the two vacua, and can be identified as the operator

\sigma\equiv v_{0}-v_{1}\,,

(VI.24)

where the coefficients of the vacua reflect the vev of
\sigma
and the action of
\eta
indeed respects equation (
VI.9
). The disorder operator
\mu
has zero vev and does not appear in the IR theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}
. Consequently, there are no operators that arise at the end of
\eta
line in the IR, and it is not possible to end the lines
1_{01},1_{10}
.

One says that the operator
\sigma
carrying generalized charge
e
is an order parameter for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric gapped phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}]
. The generalized charges of the order parameters for a symmetric gapped phase appear in the Lagrangian algebra for the corresponding physical boundary employed in the sandwich construction. Indeed, the Lagrangian algebra for the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}}
is

\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}}=1\oplus e\,.

(VI.25)

On the other hand, upto an Euler term, the TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}
is the trivial theory on which the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry is realized trivially by the identity line operator

\eta\equiv 1

(VI.26)

As a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric theory, we may represent
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}
as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}=\text{Trivial}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(VI.27)

Under the RG flow to
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}
, the spin operator
\sigma
of the Ising CFT does not acquire a non-zero vacuum expectation value (vev), and hence
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}
does not have any local operators charged non-trivially under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry. On the other hand, the disorder operator
\mu
acquires a non-zero vev appearing in the IR theory as the identity operator viewed as living at the end of the identity line
\eta
. For this reason, one says that the operator
\mu
carrying generalized charge
m
is an order parameter for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric gapped phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}]
. An order parameter in a non-trivial twisted sector is also referred to as a
string order parameter
. Thus, the phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{1}]
is described by a conventional order parameter, while the phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}]
is described by a string order parameter. Correspondingly, the Lagrangian algebra for the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}}
is

\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}}=1\oplus m\,.

(VI.28)

The boundary phase transition is implemented by deforming the conformal boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\text{Ising}}
by the operator
\epsilon_{\partial}
. On one side of the transition, the operator
\sigma_{\partial}
survives while the operator
\mu_{\partial}
does not, and hence the gapped boundary arising in the IR is described by the Lagrangian algebra (
VI.25
). On the other side of the transition, the operator
\sigma_{\partial}
does not survive while the operator
\mu_{\partial}
survives, and hence the gapped boundary arising in the IR is described by the Lagrangian algebra (
VI.28
).

VI.2.2
3-State Potts Model

We also consider a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric phase transition, which is given by the 2d RCFT called the three-state Potts model with
c=4/5
. Our focus will only be on the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry of the CFT, but more generally it is well known that the CFT admits a fusion category symmetry with 16 simple objects. See
Frohlich et al. 2004
;
Chang et al. 2019
for more details about this.

We will call the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry generating line
\eta
and focus on five special local operators in the CFT (that are all primary fields)

\sigma\,,\quad\sigma^{*}\,,\quad\mu\,,\quad\mu^{*}\,,\quad\epsilon\,,

(VI.29)

where
\sigma
is an untwisted local operator with charge 1 under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
,
\sigma^{*}
is an untwisted local operator with charge 2 under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
,
\mu
is an
\eta
-twisted sector operator uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
,
\mu^{*}
is an
\eta
-twisted sector operator uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
, and
\epsilon
is an untwisted relevant operator uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
. Collecting together, the action of
\eta
on these operators is

\eta:~\sigma\to\omega\sigma,\quad\sigma^{*}\to\omega^{2}\sigma^{*},\quad(\mu,\mu^{*},\epsilon)\to(\mu,\mu^{*},\epsilon)\,.

(VI.30)

These operators correspond to different generalized charges for the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry. As we discussed in section
III.1
, the generalized charges of a symmetry are captured by the anyons of its associated SymTFT. In the case of non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry, the SymTFT
\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}})
is the 3d Dijkgraaf-Witten discrete gauge theory based on the gauge group
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
without any twist, whose anyons are labeled as in (
IV.89
). Furthermore, the symmetry boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}
is taken to be the one corresponding to the Lagrangian algebra

\mathcal{L}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}=1\oplus e\oplus e^{2}\,.

(VI.31)

Then the generalized charges for the above operators are

q(\sigma)=e,~~q(\sigma^{*})=e^{2},~~q(\mu)=m,~~q(\mu^{*})=m^{2},~~q(\epsilon)=1\,.

(VI.32)

Deforming the CFT by the relevant operator
\epsilon
leads to
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric gapped phases in the IR, which are different depending on the sign
\pm\epsilon
of the deformation. The two gapped phases are
9
9

9

The CFT actually admits an
S_{3}
symmetry under which
\epsilon
is uncharged. We could thus also regard the 3-state Potts transition as also an
S_{3}
-symmetric transition between
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
SSB and trivial phases for
S_{3}
symmetry. The additional
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry does not get spontaneously broken on either side of the transition.

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{3}$ SSB phase for $\mathbb{Z}_{3}$ symmetry}

(VI.33)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{Trivial gapped phase for $\mathbb{Z}_{3}$ symmetry}

Upto an overall Euler term, the TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}
comprises of three universes, all occupied by the trivial theory

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}=\text{Trivial}_{0}\,\oplus\,\text{Trivial}_{1}\,\oplus\,\text{Trivial}_{2}\,,

(VI.34)

where the copies of the trivial theory are distinguished by subscript labels. The identity local operators
v_{i}
of the trivial theories satisfy product rules

v_{i}v_{j}=\delta_{ij}v_{i}\,,

(VI.35)

and are also referred to as vacua. The topological line operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}
are

1_{ij},\qquad i,j\in\{0,1,2\}\,,

(VI.36)

where
1_{ii}
is the identity line in
\text{Trivial}_{i}
, and
1_{ij}
for
i\neq j
changes
\text{Trivial}_{i}
to
\text{Trivial}_{j}
. There is no relative Euler term, and hence the linking actions of
1_{ij}
on the vacua are

1_{ij}:~v_{i}\to v_{j}\,.

(VI.37)

The fusion rules of the lines are

1_{ij}\otimes 1_{jk}=1_{ik}\,.

(VI.38)

The
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry
\eta
is identified in the IR as

\eta\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus 1_{20}\,,

(VI.39)

which cyclically permutes the three vacua

\eta:~v_{0}\to v_{1}\to v_{2}\to v_{0}\,.

(VI.40)

As a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric theory, we may represent
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}
as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}=\text{Trivial}_{0}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{1}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{2}
\mathbb{Z}_{3}

(VI.41)

The operators
\sigma
and
\sigma^{*}
of the CFT acquire non-zero vevs in the three vacua, and can be identified as the operators

\sigma\equiv v_{0}+\omega^{2}v_{1}+\omega v_{2},\quad\sigma^{*}\equiv v_{0}+\omega v_{1}+\omega^{2}v_{2}\,,

(VI.42)

where the coefficients of the vacua reflect the vevs of the operators and the action of
\eta
indeed respects equation (
VI.30
). The operators
\mu,\mu^{*}
have zero vevs and do not appear in the IR theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}
. Consequently, there are no operators that arise at the end of
\eta
and
\eta^{2}
lines in the IR, and it is not possible to end the lines
1_{ij}
for
i\neq j
.

One says that the operators
\sigma,\sigma^{*}
carrying generalized charges
e,e^{2}
are order parameters for the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric gapped phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}]
. The generalized charges of the order parameters for a symmetric gapped phase appear in the Lagrangian algebra for the corresponding physical boundary employed in the sandwich construction. Indeed, the Lagrangian algebra for the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}}
is

\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{1}}=1\oplus e\oplus e^{2}\,.

(VI.43)

On the other hand, up to an Euler term, the TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{2}
is the trivial theory on which the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry is realized trivially by the identity line operator

\eta\equiv 1\,.

(VI.44)

As a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric theory, we may represent
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{2}
as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{2}=\text{Trivial}
\mathbb{Z}_{3}

(VI.45)

Under the RG flow to
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{2}
, the operators
\sigma,\sigma^{*}
of the CFT do not acquire a non-zero vacuum expectation value (vev), and hence
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{2}
does not have any local operators charged non-trivially under the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry. On the other hand, the disorder operators
\mu,\mu^{*}
acquire non-zero vevs, appearing in the IR theory as identity operators viewed as living at the ends of the lines
\eta,\eta^{2}
. For this reason, one says that the operators
\mu,\mu^{*}
carrying generalized charges
m,m^{2}
are order parameters for the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric gapped phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{2}]
. Both of these are string order parameters. Correspondingly, the Lagrangian algebra for the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{2}}
is

\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}}_{2}}=1\oplus m\oplus m^{2}\,.

(VI.46)

VI.3
Phase Transition between
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
SSB and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB Phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
Symmetry

Let us now discuss the phase transitions obtained by applying KT transformations discussed in the previous section. First consider the KT transformation studied in section
V.2
.

This KT transformation converts
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry into
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry. Thus, we can input the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric phase transition provided by the 2d Ising CFT to obtain a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric phase transition.

The
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric gapped phases lying on the two sides of the transition are obtained as KT transformations of the SSB and Trivial phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry discussed in section
VI.2.1
. These can be quickly deduced by composing Lagrangian algebras as described in (
V.15
). This amounts to applying the map (
V.28
) to Lagrangian algebras
\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1,2}}
. We find Lagrangian algebras associated to the boundaries
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1,2}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus e\oplus e^{2}\oplus e^{3}

(VI.47)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus e^{2}\oplus m^{2}\oplus e^{2}m^{2}

Performing the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
sandwich construction with these physical boundaries, we can identify the KT transformed gapped phases as

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{4}$ SSB phase for $\mathbb{Z}_{4}$ symmetry}

(VI.48)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ SSB phase for $\mathbb{Z}_{4}$ symmetry}

Note that in the phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]
the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
remains spontaneously unbroken. See
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
for more details.

Another equivalent way to deduce these
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric gapped phases is to apply the results of section
V.2
. Applying them to the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}
appearing in (
VI.27
), we learn that the KT transformed theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
takes the form

\text{Trivial}_{0}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{1}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\quad=
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

(VI.49)

with the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry generated by

\phi(P)=1_{01}\oplus P_{10}=1_{01}\oplus(P_{11}\otimes 1_{10})\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{10}\,,

(VI.50)

where we have used the fact that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry in the trivial theory
\text{Trivial}_{1}
is generated by the identity line
1_{11}
. Moreover the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry is generated by the identity line operator as

\phi(P^{2})=P_{00}\oplus P_{11}\equiv 1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}\,.

(VI.51)

Thus
\mathfrak{T}_{2}^{\mathcal{S}}
is a 2d TQFT with two vacua on which the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
acts and the generators of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
act by exchanging the two vacua. This is precisely the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry.

Now let us consider the KT transformation of the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
appearing in (
VI.23
). We know that the KT transformed theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
has two universes of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
, i.e.

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}=(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1})_{0}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1})_{1}\,,

(VI.52)

where
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1})_{i}
denote two universes of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
. Each universe
(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1})_{i}
is further comprised of two vacua

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1})_{0}

\displaystyle=\text{Trivial}_{0}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{2}

(VI.53)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1})_{1}

\displaystyle=\text{Trivial}_{1}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{3}\,.

In total,
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
is a 2d TQFT comprising of four vacua
\text{Trivial}_{i}
, where
i\in\{0,1,2,3\}
. The line operators in such a theory are
1_{ij}
where
i,j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
with fusions

1_{ij}\otimes 1_{jk}=1_{ik}\,.

(VI.54)

The generator of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry acts by exchanging the vacua within each universe and is hence realized by line

\phi(P^{2})\equiv 1_{02}\oplus 1_{20}\oplus 1_{13}\oplus 1_{31}\,.

(VI.55)

The generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry is realized by line

\phi(P)\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus 1_{23}\oplus 1_{30}\,,

(VI.56)

which cyclically permutes the four vacua. This is precisely the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
SSB phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry.

The KT transformation of the Ising CFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}
is thus a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric CFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
providing a transition between the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry, which can be expressed as

\text{Ising}_{0}\oplus\text{Ising}_{1}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}\quad=
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

(VI.57)

where
\text{Ising}_{i}
is a copy of the Ising CFT. One may say that
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
is a gapless theory with two universes, such that each universe comprises of a copy of Ising CFT. Note that the relative Euler term between the two Ising universes is trivial.

The relevant topological line operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
are

1_{ij},\quad\eta_{ij}\,,

(VI.58)

where
1_{ii}
is the identity line of
\text{Ising}_{i}
,
\eta_{ii}
is the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
spin flip symmetry of
\text{Ising}_{i}
, the lines
1_{01},1_{10}
exchange the two universes
\text{Ising}_{0}
and
\text{Ising}_{1}
, and the lines
\eta_{01},\eta_{10}
are obtained as

\displaystyle\eta_{01}

\displaystyle:=1_{01}\otimes\eta_{11}=\eta_{00}\otimes 1_{01}

(VI.59)

\displaystyle\eta_{10}

\displaystyle:=1_{10}\otimes\eta_{00}=\eta_{11}\otimes 1_{10}\,.

The generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry is realized as the line operator

\phi(P)=1_{01}\oplus\eta_{10}\,.

(VI.60)

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
is identified as the diagonal
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
of the two
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
spin flip symmetries acting on the two Ising universes, i.e.

\phi(P^{2})=\eta_{00}\oplus\eta_{11}\,.

(VI.61)

The action of the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry on the local operators living in the two Ising universes is

P:\quad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{0}&\to\sigma_{1},\\
\mu_{0}&\to\mu_{1},\\
\epsilon_{0}&\to\epsilon_{1},\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{1}&\to-\sigma_{0}\\
\mu_{1}&\to\mu_{0}\\
\epsilon_{1}&\to\epsilon_{0}\,.\end{aligned}

(VI.62)

The relevant operator responsible for the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric transition is
\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}
. Indeed, one side of the deformation sends
\text{Ising}_{i}
to the trivial phase
\text{Trivial}_{i}
for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry
\eta_{ii}
, thus in total realizing the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry; while the other side of the deformation sends
\text{Ising}_{i}
to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry
\eta_{ii}
, thus in total realizing the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry.

From equations (
V.29
) and (
V.30
), we learn that the order parameters for the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
are realized by local operators

{\mathcal{O}}_{e}=\sigma_{0}-i\sigma_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}}=\text{id}_{0}-\text{id}_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{3}}=\sigma_{0}+i\sigma_{1}\,,

(VI.63)

where
\text{id}_{i}
is the identity local operator of
\mathsf{Ising}_{i}
. The generalized charge carried by operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{p}}
is
e^{p}
, which means that it is an untwisted sector local operator with charge
p
under the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry. After the RG flow, these operators are realized in the IR as

\begin{aligned} \text{id}_{0}&\equiv v_{0}+v_{2},\\
\text{id}_{1}&\equiv v_{1}+v_{3},\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{0}&\equiv v_{0}-v_{2}\\
\sigma_{1}&\equiv v_{1}-v_{3}\,,\end{aligned}

(VI.64)

where
v_{i}
are the vacua or in other words the identity local operators of the IR theories
\text{Trivial}_{i}
. This follows from (
VI.24
). From this, we learn that the IR images of the above order parameters is

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{e}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}-iv_{1}-v_{2}+iv_{3}

(VI.65)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}-v_{1}+v_{2}-v_{3}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{3}}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+iv_{1}-v_{2}-iv_{3}\,.

Similarly, from equations (
V.29
) and (
V.31
), the order parameters for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}_{2}^{\mathcal{S}}]
for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry are realized by local operators

{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}=\mu_{0}+\mu_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}}=\text{id}_{0}-\text{id}_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}m^{2}}=\mu_{0}-\mu_{1}\,.

(VI.66)

The subscripts of the operators describe their generalized charges. Here
{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}m^{2}}
are in the
P^{2}
-twisted sector and hence are string order parameters, coming attached to line
\eta_{00}\oplus\eta_{11}
. After the RG flow, these operators are realized in the IR as

\text{id}_{0}\equiv v_{0},\quad\text{id}_{1}\equiv v_{1},\quad\mu_{0}\equiv v_{0},\quad\mu_{1}\equiv v_{1}\,,

(VI.67)

where
v_{i}
are the vacua of the IR theories
\text{Trivial}_{i}
. From this, we learn that the IR images of the above order parameters is

{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}\equiv v_{0}+v_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}}\equiv v_{0}-v_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}m^{2}}\equiv v_{0}-v_{1}\,,

(VI.68)

where
{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}m^{2}}
are viewed as local operators living at the ends of the line
\phi(P^{2})
whose IR image is the identity line
1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}
.

VI.4
Phase Transition between
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and Trivial Phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
Symmetry

Now consider the KT transformation studied in section
V.2
.

This KT transformation also converts
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry to
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry, but this time it is done using pullback via the non-trivial homomorphism
\mathbb{Z}_{4}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. We again use the Ising CFT to obtain a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric phase transition described below.

This KT transformation simply amounts to regarding the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry as the generator of a new
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry. Thus the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry becomes the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry, which is the phase in which the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
is spontaneously broken but the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
is preserved. On the other hand, the trivial phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
becomes the trivial phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
. That is the gapped phases on the two sides of the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric transition are

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ SSB phase for $\mathbb{Z}_{4}$ symmetry}

(VI.69)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{Trivial phase for $\mathbb{Z}_{4}$ symmetry}

This can also be seen by composing with the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{I}}
shown in (
IV.57
), after which we obtain the Lagrangian algebras

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus e^{2}\oplus m^{2}\oplus e^{2}m^{2}

(VI.70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus m\oplus m^{2}\oplus m^{3}\,,

which precisely correspond to the above quoted gapped phases.

The
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric phase transition is simply the Ising CFT regarded as a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetric theory

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}=\text{Ising}
\mathbb{Z}_{4}

(VI.71)

with the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
being realized by the
\eta
line

\phi(P)=\eta\,.

(VI.72)

The relevant operator responsible for the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
-symmetric transition is
\epsilon
. From equations (
V.39
) and (
V.40
), we learn that the order parameters for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
are realized by local operators

{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}}=\sigma,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}=\text{id},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}m^{2}}=\sigma\,,

(VI.73)

where
{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}
is the identity local operator viewed as lying in the
P^{2}
-twisted sector, and
{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}m^{2}}
is the
\sigma
operator viewed as lying in the
P^{2}
-twisted sector. Using the IR image (
VI.24
) of
\sigma
, the IR images of above operators are

{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}}=v_{0}-v_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}=1=v_{0}+v_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e^{2}m^{2}}=v_{0}-v_{1}\,.

(VI.74)

Similarly, from equations (
V.39
) and (
V.41
), the order parameters for the trivial phase
[\mathfrak{T}_{2}^{\mathcal{S}}]
for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry are realized by local operators

{\mathcal{O}}_{m}=\mu,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}=\text{id},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{3}}=\mu\,,

(VI.75)

where
{\mathcal{O}}_{m},{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{3}}
are
\mu
operators viewed as lying in the
P,P^{3}
-twisted sectors respectively. The IR images of the above operators are

{\mathcal{O}}_{m}\equiv{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{2}}\equiv{\mathcal{O}}_{m^{3}}\equiv 1\,.

(VI.76)

The identity local operator can be viewed to be living in any
P^{k}
-twisted sector because the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry in the trivial phase is realized by the identity line.

VI.5
Phase Transition between
S_{3}
SSB and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB Phases for
S_{3}
Symmetry

Now consider the KT transformation studied in section
V.5
. This KT transformation converts
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry to
S_{3}
symmetry. Thus, we can input the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
-symmetric phase transition provided by the 3-State Potts Model to obtain an
S_{3}
-symmetric phase transition.

The
S_{3}
-symmetric gapped phases lying on the two sides of the transition are obtained as KT transformations of the SSB and Trivial phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry discussed in section
VI.2.2
. These can be quickly deduced by composing Lagrangian algebras as described in (
V.15
). This amounts to applying the map (
V.62
) to Lagrangian algebras
\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1,2}}
. We find Lagrangian algebras associated to the boundaries
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1,2}}
to be

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus 2E

(VI.77)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus 2a_{1}\,.

Performing the
S_{3}
sandwich construction with these physical boundaries, we can identify the KT transformed gapped phases as

\displaystyle=\text{$S_{3}$ SSB phase for $S_{3}$ symmetry}

(VI.78)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ SSB phase for $S_{3}$ symmetry}

Note that in the phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]
the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
remains spontaneously unbroken in both the vacua. See
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
for more details.

Another equivalent way to deduce these
S_{3}
-symmetric gapped phases is to apply the results of section
V.5
. Applying them to the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}
appearing in (
VI.45
), we learn that the KT transformed theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
takes the form

\text{Trivial}_{0}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{1}
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\quad=
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(VI.79)

with the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
generated by IR line

a\equiv 1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}

(VI.80)

and any
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
S_{3}
generated by IR line

a^{i}b\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{10}\,.

(VI.81)

Thus
\mathfrak{T}_{2}^{\mathcal{S}}
is a 2d TQFT with two vacua on which the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
acts trivially, and all
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroups of
S_{3}
exchange the two vacua. This is precisely the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
S_{3}
symmetry.

Now let us consider the KT transformation of the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
appearing in (
VI.41
). We know that
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
comprises of three vacua, and since
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
comprises of two universes of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, the 2d TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
comprises of six vacua. The only irreducible
S_{3}
-symmetric (1+1)d gapped phase with six vacua is the
S_{3}
SSB phase. The reader can easily check that the
S_{3}
symmetry indeed acts as on the
S_{3}
SSB phase.

The KT transformation of the 3-state Potts CFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}
is thus an
S_{3}
-symmetric CFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
providing a transition between the
S_{3}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phases for
S_{3}
symmetry, which can be expressed as

\text{3-Potts}_{0}\oplus\text{3-Potts}_{1}
\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{-1}
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\quad=
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(VI.82)

where
\text{3-Potts}_{i}
is a copy of the 3-state Potts CFT. One may say that
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
is a gapless theory with two universes, such that each universe comprises of a copy of 3-state Potts CFT. Note that the relative Euler term between the two Potts universes is trivial.

The
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
symmetry is realized as

a=\eta_{00}\oplus\eta_{11}^{2}\,,

(VI.83)

where
\eta_{ii}
is the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry of
\text{3-Potts}_{i}
. The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
S_{3}
symmetry generated by
b
is realized as

b=1_{01}\oplus 1_{10}\,.

(VI.84)

The relevant operator responsible for the
S_{3}
-symmetric transition is
\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}
. Indeed, one side of the deformation sends
\text{3-Potts}_{i}
to the trivial phase
\text{Trivial}_{i}
for
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry
\eta_{ii}
, thus in total realizing the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
for
S_{3}
symmetry; while the other side of the deformation sends
\text{3-Potts}_{i}
to the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
SSB phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry
\eta_{ii}
, thus in total realizing the
S_{3}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
for
S_{3}
symmetry.

The order parameters for the
S_{3}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
involve a local operator in representation
1_{-}
of
S_{3}
and two distinct multiplets of local operators in representation
E
of
S_{3}
. Applying KT transformations (
V.63
), (
V.64
) and (
V.65
) respectively to 1,
\sigma
and
\sigma^{*}
operators, we deduce that these order parameters are

{\mathcal{O}}_{1-}=\text{id}_{0}-\text{id}_{1},\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,1}=\sigma_{0},\quad&{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,2}=\sigma_{1}\\
{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,1}=\sigma^{*}_{0},\quad&{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,2}=\sigma^{*}_{1}\,,\end{aligned}

(VI.85)

where
\text{id}_{i}
is the identity local operator of
\text{3-Potts}_{i}
. Here
\{{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,1},{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,2}\}
for
k=1,2
are the two multiplets transforming in
E
representation and
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
transforms in
1_{-}
representation. After the RG flow, these operators are realized in the IR as

\begin{aligned} \text{id}_{0}&\equiv v_{0}+v_{1}+v_{2},\\
\sigma_{0}&\equiv v_{0}+\omega^{2}v_{1}+\omega v_{2},\\
\sigma^{*}_{0}&\equiv v_{0}+\omega v_{1}+\omega^{2}v_{2},\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \text{id}_{1}&\equiv v_{3}+v_{4}+v_{5}\\
\sigma_{1}&\equiv v_{3}+\omega^{2}v_{4}+\omega v_{5}\\
\sigma^{*}_{1}&\equiv v_{3}+\omega v_{4}+\omega^{2}v_{5}\,,\end{aligned}

(VI.86)

where
v_{i}
are the vacua or in other words the identity local operators of the IR theories
\text{Trivial}_{i}
comprising the
S_{3}
SSB phase. This follows from (
VI.42
). From this, we learn that the IR images of the above order parameters are

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,1}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+\omega^{2}v_{1}+\omega v_{2}

(VI.87)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,2}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+\omega v_{1}+\omega^{2}v_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+v_{1}+v_{2}-v_{3}-v_{4}-v_{5}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,1}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{3}+\omega^{2}v_{4}+\omega v_{5}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,2}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{3}+\omega v_{4}+\omega^{2}v_{5}\,.

Similarly, the order parameters for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
involve a local operator in representation
1_{-}
of
S_{3}
and two distinct multiplets of local operators in
a_{1}
-multiplet, each comprising of two operators in
a,a^{2}
-twisted sectors charged trivially under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
. Applying KT transformations (
V.63
), (
V.66
) and (
V.67
) respectively to 1,
\mu
and
\mu^{*}
operators, we deduce that these order parameters are

{\mathcal{O}}_{1-}=\text{id}_{0}-\text{id}_{1},\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a,1}=\mu_{0},\quad&{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{2},1}=\mu_{1}\\
{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a,1}=\mu^{*}_{0},\quad&{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a^{2},1}=\mu^{*}_{1}\,.\end{aligned}

(VI.88)

Here
\{{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a,1},{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a^{2},1}\}
for
k=1,2
are the two
a_{1}
-multiplets and
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
transforms in
1_{-}
representation. After the RG flow, these operators are realized in the IR as

\begin{aligned} \text{id}_{0}&\equiv v_{0},\\
\mu_{0}&\equiv v_{0},\\
\mu^{*}_{0}&\equiv v_{0},\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \text{id}_{1}&\equiv v_{1}\\
\mu_{1}&\equiv v_{1}\\
\mu^{*}_{1}&\equiv v_{1}\,.\end{aligned}

(VI.89)

where
v_{i}
are the vacua or in other words the identity local operators of the IR theories
\text{Trivial}_{i}
comprising the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase. From this, we learn that the IR images of the above order parameters are

{\mathcal{O}}_{1-}=v_{0}-v_{1},\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a,1}=v_{0},\quad&{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{2},1}=v_{1}\\
{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a,1}=v_{0},\quad&{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a^{2},1}=v_{1}\,.\end{aligned}

(VI.90)

VI.6
Phase Transition between
S_{3}
SSB and
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
SSB Phases for
S_{3}
Symmetry

Now consider the KT transformation studied in section
V.6
. This KT transformation converts
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry to
S_{3}
symmetry. Thus, we can input the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric phase transition provided by the 2d Ising CFT to obtain an
S_{3}
-symmetric phase transition.

The
S_{3}
-symmetric gapped phases lying on the two sides of the transition are obtained as KT transformations of the SSB and Trivial phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry discussed in section
VI.2.1
. These can be quickly deduced by composing Lagrangian algebras as described in (
V.15
). This amounts to applying the map (
V.71
) to Lagrangian algebras
\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1,2}}
. We find Lagrangian algebras associated to the boundaries
\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1,2}}
to be

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus 2E

(VI.91)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus E\oplus b_{+}\,.

Performing the
S_{3}
sandwich construction with these physical boundaries, we can identify the KT transformed gapped phases as

\displaystyle=\text{$S_{3}$ SSB phase for $S_{3}$ symmetry}

(VI.92)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{3}$ SSB phase for $S_{3}$ symmetry}

Note that in the phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]
a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
S_{3}
remains spontaneously unbroken in each of the three vacua. See
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
for more details.

Another equivalent way to deduce these
S_{3}
-symmetric gapped phases is to apply the results of section
V.6
. Applying them to the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}
appearing in (
VI.27
), we learn that the KT transformed theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
takes the form

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}=\text{Trivial}_{0}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{1}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{2}
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(VI.93)

with the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
generated by IR line

a\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus 1_{20}

(VI.94)

and the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
S_{3}
associated to
b
generated by IR line

b\equiv 1_{00}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus 1_{21}\,.

(VI.95)

Thus
\mathfrak{T}_{2}^{\mathcal{S}}
is a 2d TQFT with three vacua on which the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
acts by cyclic permutations, and the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
S_{3}
generated by
b
preserves a vacuum while exchanging the other two. This is precisely the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
SSB phase for
S_{3}
symmetry.

Now let us consider the KT transformation of the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
appearing in (
VI.23
). We know that
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
comprises of two vacua, and since
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
comprises of three universes of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
, the 2d TQFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}
comprises of six vacua. The only irreducible
S_{3}
-symmetric (1+1)d gapped phase with six vacua is the
S_{3}
SSB phase. The reader can easily check that the
S_{3}
symmetry indeed acts as on the
S_{3}
SSB phase.

The KT transformation of the Ising CFT
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}
is thus an
S_{3}
-symmetric CFT
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
providing a transition between the
S_{3}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
SSB phases for
S_{3}
symmetry, which can be expressed as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}=\text{Ising}_{0}\oplus\text{Ising}_{1}\oplus\text{Ising}_{2}
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(VI.96)

where
\text{Ising}_{i}
is a copy of the Ising CFT. One may say that
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
is a gapless theory with three universes, such that each universe comprises of a copy of Ising CFT. Note that the relative Euler term between the three Ising universes is trivial.

The relevant topological line operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
are

1_{ij},\quad\eta_{ij}\,,

(VI.97)

where
1_{ii}
is the identity line of
\text{Ising}_{i}
,
\eta_{ii}
is the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
spin flip symmetry of
\text{Ising}_{i}
, the lines
1_{ij}
for
i\neq j
transform
\text{Ising}_{i}
into
\text{Ising}_{j}
, and the lines
\eta_{ij}
are obtained as

\eta_{ij}:=1_{ij}\otimes\eta_{jj}=\eta_{ii}\otimes 1_{ij}\,,

(VI.98)

which also transform
\text{Ising}_{i}
into
\text{Ising}_{j}
.
The lines generating
S_{3}
symmetry are realized as

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=1_{01}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus 1_{20}

(VI.99)

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\eta_{00}\oplus\eta_{12}\oplus\eta_{21}\,.

The action of the
S_{3}
symmetry on the local operators living in
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
is

a:\quad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{0}&\to\sigma_{1},\\
\mu_{0}&\to\mu_{1},\\
\epsilon_{0}&\to\epsilon_{1},\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{1}&\to\sigma_{2}\\
\mu_{1}&\to\mu_{2}\\
\epsilon_{1}&\to\epsilon_{2}\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{2}&\to\sigma_{0}\\
\mu_{2}&\to\mu_{0}\\
\epsilon_{2}&\to\epsilon_{0}\end{aligned}

(VI.100)

and

b:\quad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{0}&\to-\sigma_{0},\\
\mu_{0}&\to\mu_{0},\\
\epsilon_{0}&\to\epsilon_{0},\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{1}&\to-\sigma_{2}\\
\mu_{1}&\to\mu_{2}\\
\epsilon_{1}&\to\epsilon_{2}\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{2}&\to-\sigma_{1}\\
\mu_{2}&\to\mu_{1}\\
\epsilon_{2}&\to\epsilon_{1}\,.\end{aligned}

(VI.101)

The relevant operator responsible for the
S_{3}
-symmetric transition is the KT transformation of
\epsilon
which according to (
V.73
) is
\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}
. Indeed, one side of the deformation sends
\text{Ising}_{i}
to the trivial phase
\text{Trivial}_{i}
for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry
\eta_{ii}
, thus in total realizing the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
for
S_{3}
symmetry; while the other side of the deformation sends
\text{Ising}_{i}
to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry
\eta_{ii}
, thus in total realizing the
S_{3}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
for
S_{3}
symmetry.

The order parameters for the
S_{3}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
involve a local operator in representation
1_{-}
of
S_{3}
and two distinct multiplets of local operators in representation
E
of
S_{3}
. Applying KT transformations (
V.73
) and (
V.74
) respectively to the identity operator 1 and the spin operator
\sigma
of the Ising CFT, we deduce that these order parameters are

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,1}

\displaystyle=\text{id}_{0}+\omega^{2}\text{id}_{1}+\omega\text{id}_{2}

(VI.102)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,2}

\displaystyle=\text{id}_{0}+\omega\text{id}_{1}+\omega^{2}\text{id}_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{0}+\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,1}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{0}+\omega^{2}\sigma_{1}+\omega\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,2}

\displaystyle=-\sigma_{0}-\omega\sigma_{1}-\omega^{2}\sigma_{2}

where
\text{id}_{i}
is the identity local operator of
\text{Ising}_{i}
. Here
\{{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,1},{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,2}\}
for
k=1,2
are the two multiplets transforming in
E
representation and
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
transforms in
1_{-}
representation. After the RG flow, these operators are realized in the IR as

\begin{aligned} \text{id}_{0}&\equiv v_{0}+v_{3},\\
\text{id}_{1}&\equiv v_{1}+v_{4},\\
\text{id}_{2}&\equiv v_{2}+v_{5},\end{aligned}\qquad\begin{aligned} \sigma_{0}&\equiv v_{0}-v_{3}\\
\sigma_{1}&\equiv v_{1}-v_{4}\\
\sigma_{2}&\equiv v_{2}-v_{5}\end{aligned}

(VI.103)

where
v_{i}
are the vacua or in other words the identity local operators of the IR theories
\text{Trivial}_{i}
comprising the
S_{3}
SSB phase. This follows from (
VI.24
). From this, we learn that the IR images of the above order parameters is

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,1}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+\omega^{2}v_{1}+\omega v_{2}+v_{3}+\omega^{2}v_{4}+\omega v_{5}

(VI.104)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,2}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+\omega v_{1}+\omega^{2}v_{2}+v_{3}+\omega v_{4}+\omega^{2}v_{5}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+v_{1}+v_{2}-v_{3}-v_{4}-v_{5}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,1}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+\omega^{2}v_{1}+\omega v_{2}-v_{3}-\omega^{2}v_{4}-\omega v_{5}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,2}

\displaystyle\equiv-v_{0}-\omega v_{1}-\omega^{2}v_{2}+v_{3}+\omega v_{4}+\omega^{2}v_{5}\,.

Similarly, the order parameters for the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
involve two local operators in representation
E
of
S_{3}
and three local operators in
b_{+}
-multiplet of
S_{3}
in which the three operators are in twisted sectors for generators of the three
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroups of
S_{3}
respectively and are uncharged under those
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroups. Applying KT transformations (
V.73
) and (
V.76
) to the identity operator 1 and the disorder operator
\mu
of the Ising CFT, we deduce that these order parameters are

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,1}

\displaystyle=\text{id}_{0}+\omega^{2}\text{id}_{1}+\omega\text{id}_{2}

(VI.105)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,2}

\displaystyle=\text{id}_{0}+\omega\text{id}_{1}+\omega^{2}\text{id}_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a^{i}b}

\displaystyle=\mu_{i},\qquad i\in\{0,1,2\}\,.

Here
{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,1},{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,2}
are operators transforming in
E
representation and
{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a^{i}b}
are operators transforming as a
b_{+}
-multiplet. After the RG flow, these operators are realized in the IR as

\text{id}_{i}\equiv v_{i},\qquad\mu_{i}\equiv v_{i}\,,

(VI.106)

where
v_{i}
are the vacua of the IR theories
\text{Trivial}_{i}
. From this, we learn that the IR images of the above order parameters is

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,1}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+\omega^{2}v_{1}+\omega v_{2}

(VI.107)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,2}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{0}+\omega v_{1}+\omega^{2}v_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a^{i}b}

\displaystyle\equiv v_{i},\qquad i\in\{0,1,2\}\,,

where
{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a^{i}b}
are viewed as operators in the
a^{i}b
twisted sector.

VI.7
Phase Transition between
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and Trivial Phases for
S_{3}
Symmetry

Now consider the KT transformation studied in section
V.7
. This KT transformation also converts
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry to
S_{3}
symmetry, but this time it is done using pullback via the non-trivial homomorphism
S_{3}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. We again use the Ising CFT to obtain an
S_{3}
-symmetric phase transition described below.

This KT transformation simply amounts to regarding the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry as the generators for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroups inside
S_{3}
, and the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
acts trivially. Thus the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry becomes the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
S_{3}
symmetry. On the other hand, the trivial phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
becomes the trivial phase for
S_{3}
. That is the gapped phases on the two sides of the
S_{3}
-symmetric transition are

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ SSB phase for $S_{3}$ symmetry}

(VI.108)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{Trivial phase for $S_{3}$ symmetry}

This can also be seen by using the map (
V.80
) to deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus 2a_{1}

(VI.109)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus a_{1}\oplus b_{+}\,,

which precisely correspond to the above quoted gapped phases.

The
S_{3}
-symmetric phase transition is simply the Ising CFT regarded as an
S_{3}
symmetric theory

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}=\text{Ising}
S_{3}

(VI.110)

with the generators of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroups of
S_{3}
being realized by the
\eta
line, while all the other elements being realized by the trivial line

b=ab=a^{2}b=\eta\,.

(VI.111)

The relevant operator responsible for the
S_{3}
-symmetric transition is
\epsilon
. The order parameters for the
Z_{2}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
involve a local operator in representation
1_{-}
of
S_{3}
and two distinct
a_{1}
-multiplets of local operators, each comprising of two operators in
a,a^{2}
-twisted sectors charged trivially under
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of
S_{3}
. Applying KT transformations (
V.81
) and (
V.82
) respectively to the identity operator
id
and the spin operator
\sigma
of the Ising CFT, we deduce that these order parameters are

{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{i}}=\text{id},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}=\sigma,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a^{i}}=(-1)^{i}\sigma

(VI.112)

for
i=1,2
. Here
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a^{i}}
for
k=1,2
are the two
a_{1}
-multiplets and
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
is the operator in
1_{-}
representation of
S_{3}
. Using the IR image (
VI.24
) of
\sigma
, the IR images of above operators are

{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{i}}\equiv v_{0}+v_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}\equiv v_{0}-v_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a^{i}}\equiv(-1)^{i}(v_{0}-v_{1})\,.

(VI.113)

Similarly, the order parameters for the trivial phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
involve an
a_{1}
-multiplet and a
b_{+}
-multiplet. Applying KT transformations (
V.81
) and (
V.83
) respectively to the identity operator
id
and the disorder operator
\mu
of the Ising CFT, we deduce that these order parameters are

{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{i}}=\text{id},\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a^{k}b}=\mu

(VI.114)

for
i=1,2
parametrizing operators in
a_{1}
multiplet and
k=0,1,2
parametrizing operators in
b_{+}
multiplet. The IR images of the above operators are

{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a^{i}}\equiv 1,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{a^{i}b}\equiv 1\,,

(VI.115)

where 1 is the identity operator of the trivial phase.

VI.8
Phase Transition between
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and Trivial Phases for
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
Symmetry

Now consider the KT transformation studied in section
V.8
. This KT transformation converts
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry to
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. We use the 3-state Potts CFT to obtain a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric phase transition described below.

Using the map (
V.87
), we can quickly deduce that the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases obtained after applying KT transformation on
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
SSB and trivial phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry are described respectively by the physical Lagrangian algebras

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus 2a_{1}

(VI.116)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus 2E\,,

which correspond to the following
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases respectively

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ SSB phase for $\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})$ symmetry}

(VI.117)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{Trivial phase for $\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})$ symmetry}

For more details on these gapped phases, we refer the reader to
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
.

Another equivalent way to deduce these
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases is to apply the results of section
V.8
. Applying them to the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}
appearing in (
VI.45
), we learn that the KT transformed theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
takes the form

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\text{Trivial}
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})

(VI.118)

which means that
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
is an SPT phase for
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry since it involves a single vacuum, but there is only one such SPT phase that is often referred to as the trivial
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric phase.

Now let us consider the KT transformation of the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
appearing in (
VI.41
). We know that
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
comprises of three vacua, and hence
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
contains
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry realized on a (1+1)d gapped phase with three vacua. There are two possible options for such a phase, namely
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
SSB phases. Since the three vacua of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
are identified as the three vacua of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
, we learn that the relative Euler terms between the three vacua of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}
must all be trivial. This fixes
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}
to be the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase. Using (
IV.147
), we can identify the lines implementing
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
as

\displaystyle 1_{-}

\displaystyle\equiv 1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}\oplus 1_{22}

(VI.119)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus 1_{20}\oplus 1_{02}\oplus 1_{10}\oplus 1_{21}\,,

which matches the results of
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
.

The
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric phase transition is simply the 3-state Potts CFT regarded as a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetric theory

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}=\text{3-Potts}
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})

(VI.120)

with the generators of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
being realized as

1_{-}=1,\qquad E=\eta\oplus\eta^{2}\,.

(VI.121)

The relevant operator responsible for the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric transition is
\epsilon
.

The order parameters for the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/Z_{2}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
involve a
1_{-}
-multiplet and two distinct
a_{1}
-multiplets of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
, whose detailed form was discussed above (
V.88
) and (
V.89
) respectively. Applying KT transformations (
V.88
) and (
V.89
) to the operators
id
,
\sigma
and
\sigma^{*}
of the 3-state Potts CFT, we deduce that these order parameters are

{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}=\text{id},~~{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},u}=\sigma,~~{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},t}=\sigma,~~{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a_{1},u}=\sigma^{*},~~{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a_{1},t}=\sigma^{*}

(VI.122)

where
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
forms the
1_{-}
-multiplet and
\{{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{1},u},{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{a_{1},t}\}
for
k=1,2
are the two
a_{1}
-multiplets. The IR images of above operators are

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}

\displaystyle=v_{0}+v_{1}+v_{2}

(VI.123)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},u}

\displaystyle=v_{0}+\omega^{2}v_{1}+\omega v_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{a_{1},t}

\displaystyle=v_{0}+\omega^{2}v_{1}+\omega v_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a_{1},u}

\displaystyle=v_{0}+\omega v_{1}+\omega^{2}v_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{a_{1},t}

\displaystyle=v_{0}+\omega v_{1}+\omega^{2}v_{2}

The order parameters for the trivial phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
involve a
1_{-}
-multiplet and two distinct
E
-multiplets of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
, whose detailed form was discussed above (
V.88
) and (
V.90
) respectively. Applying KT transformations (
V.88
) and (
V.90
) to the operators
id
,
\mu
and
\mu^{*}
of the 3-state Potts CFT, we deduce that these order parameters are are

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,+}=\mu,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,-}=\mu,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}=\text{id},\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,+}=\mu^{*}

(VI.124)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,-}=\mu^{*}\,.

Here
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,\pm}
for
k=1,2
form two
E
-multiplets and
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
forms a
1_{-}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
. The IR images of above operators are

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,+}\equiv 1,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,-}\equiv 1,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}\equiv 1,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,+}\equiv 1

(VI.125)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(2)}_{E,-}\equiv 1\,.

That is, the above operators are simply the identity operator viewed in different fashions as transitioning between different line operators associated to the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. This result is not surprising as identity operator is the only operator available in the trivial phase.

VI.9
Phase Transition between
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and Trivial Phases for
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
Symmetry

Now consider the KT transformation studied in section
V.9
. This KT transformation converts
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry to
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. We use the Ising CFT to obtain a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric phase transition described below.

Using the map (
V.95
), we can quickly deduce that the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases obtained after applying KT transformation on
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and trivial phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry are described respectively by the physical Lagrangian algebras

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus E\oplus b_{+}

(VI.126)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus 2E\,,

which correspond to the following
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases respectively

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ SSB phase for $\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})$ symmetry}

(VI.127)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{Trivial phase for $\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})$ symmetry}

For more details on these gapped phases, we refer the reader to
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
.

Another equivalent way to deduce these
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases is to apply the results of section
V.9
. Applying them to the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}
appearing in (
VI.27
), we learn that the KT transformed theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
takes the form

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\text{Trivial}
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})

(VI.128)

which means that
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
is the trivial
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric phase.

Now let us consider the KT transformation of the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
appearing in (
VI.23
). We know that
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
comprises of two vacua, and hence
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
contains
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry realized on a 2d TQFT with two vacua, which fixes
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase for
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. Using (
IV.139
), we can identify the lines implementing
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
as

1_{-}\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{10},\quad E\equiv 1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}\oplus 1_{01}\oplus 1_{10}\,,

(VI.129)

which matches the results of
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
.

The
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric phase transition is simply the Ising CFT regarded as a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetric theory

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}=\text{Ising}
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})

(VI.130)

with the generators of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
being realized as

1_{-}=\eta,\qquad E=1\oplus\eta\,.

(VI.131)

The relevant operator responsible for the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric transition is
\epsilon
.

The order parameters for the
Z_{2}
SSB phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
involve an
E
-multiplet and a
b_{+}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
, whose detailed form was discussed above (
V.90
) and (
V.98
) respectively. Applying KT transformations (
V.96
) and (
V.98
) respectively to the identity operator
id
and the spin operator
\sigma
of the Ising CFT, we deduce that these order parameters are

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,+}=\text{id},\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,-}=\text{id}_{\eta},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+}}=\sigma,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},+}=\sigma

(VI.132)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},-}=\sigma\otimes\text{id}_{\eta}\,,

where
\text{id}_{\eta}
is the identity local operator on the
\eta
line. Here
{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,\pm}
form an
E
-multiplet and
{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+}},{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},\pm}
form a
b_{+}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
. The IR images of above operators are

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,+}\equiv v_{0}+v_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,-}\equiv\text{id}_{01}+\text{id}_{10},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+}}\equiv v_{0}-v_{1}

(VI.133)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},+}\equiv v_{0}-v_{1},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{b_{+},-}\equiv\text{id}_{01}-\text{id}_{10}\,,

where
\text{id}_{ij}
is the identity local operator on the
1_{ij}
vacua changing line.

The order parameters for the trivial phase
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
involve a
1_{-}
-multiplet and two distinct
E
-multiplets of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
, whose detailed form was discussed above (
V.88
) and (
V.90
) respectively. Applying KT transformations (
V.96
) and (
V.97
) respectively to the identity operator
id
and the disorder operator
\mu
of the Ising CFT, we deduce that these order parameters are

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,+}=\text{id},\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,-}=\text{id}_{P},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}=\mu,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,+}=\mu

(VI.134)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,-}=\mu\,.

Here
{\mathcal{O}}^{(k)}_{E,\pm}
for
k=0,1
form two
E
-multiplets and
{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}
forms a
1_{-}
-multiplet of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
. The IR images of above operators are

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,+}\equiv 1,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)}_{E,-}\equiv 1,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{1_{-}}\equiv 1,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,+}\equiv 1

(VI.135)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{E,-}\equiv 1\,.

That is, the above operators are simply the identity operator viewed in different fashions as transitioning between different line operators associated to the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. This result is not surprising as identity operator is the only operator available in the trivial phase.

VI.10
Phase Transition between
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
SSB Phases for
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
Symmetry

Now consider the KT transformation studied in section
V.10
. This KT transformation converts
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry to
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry. We again use the Ising CFT to obtain a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric phase transition described below.

Using the map (
V.107
), we can quickly deduce that the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases obtained after applying KT transformation on
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and trivial phases for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry are described respectively by the physical Lagrangian algebras

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus 1_{-}\oplus 2a_{1}

(VI.136)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{phys}}_{\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1\oplus a_{1}\oplus b_{+}

which correspond to the following
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases respectively

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ SSB phase for $\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})$ symmetry}

(VI.137)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{$\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})$ SSB phase for $\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})$ symmetry}

For more details on these gapped phases, we refer the reader to
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
.

Another equivalent way to deduce these
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases is to apply the results of section
V.10
. Applying them to the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
appearing in (
VI.23
), we learn that the universes involved in the KT transformed theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
are

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1})_{e}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Z}_{2}\text{ SSB}=\text{Trivial}_{0}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{1}

(VI.138)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1})_{m}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\text{ Trivial}=\text{Trivial}_{2}\,,

where
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
denotes the dual symmetry obtained after gauging the original
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{1}
. Thus in total,
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
is a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric (1+1)d gapped phase with three vacua. Now the question arises which one it is, because there are two possible
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric gapped phases having three vacua, namely the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB and
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
SSB phases (see
Bhardwaj et al. 2025
for more details). In order to answer this, we need to understand the precise form of the line operators implementing the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry on
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
, which follows from (
IV.159
). The identification of the lines appearing there is

\begin{aligned} 1_{ee}&\equiv 1_{00}\oplus 1_{11},\\
P_{ee}&\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{10},\end{aligned}\,\quad\begin{aligned} 1_{mm}&\equiv 1_{22}\\
P_{mm}&\equiv 1_{22}\end{aligned}\,\quad\begin{aligned} S_{em}&\equiv 1_{02}\oplus 1_{12},\\
S_{me}&\equiv 1_{20}\oplus 1_{21}\,.\end{aligned}

(VI.139)

This implies that the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry generators are identified as

\displaystyle 1_{-}

\displaystyle\equiv 1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}\oplus 1_{22}

(VI.140)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\equiv 1_{02}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus 1_{20}\oplus 1_{21}\oplus 1_{01}\oplus 1_{10}\,.

Note that there are no relative Euler terms between the three vacua since the linking action (
V.104
) becomes

S_{em}:~v_{0}+v_{1}\to 2v_{2}\,\qquad S_{me}:~v_{2}\to v_{0}+v_{1}\,,

(VI.141)

which means that the linking actions of the vacua changing lines are

\begin{aligned} 1_{02}&:~v_{0}\to v_{2},\\
1_{12}&:~v_{1}\to v_{2},\end{aligned}\,\qquad\begin{aligned} 1_{20}&:~v_{2}\to v_{0}\\
1_{21}&:~v_{2}\to v_{1}\end{aligned}

(VI.142)

implying that there are no relative Euler terms between the three vacua. All these properties imply that
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{1}]
is the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase. In particular, note that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subsymmetry of
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
remains spontaneously unbroken as we have found in (
VI.140
) that
1_{-}
is realized by the identity line operator. The three vacua are thus mixed into each other solely by the action of the non-invertible symmetry
E
.

Applying the results of section
V.10
to the theory
\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{2}
appearing in (
VI.27
), we learn that the universes involved in the KT transformed theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
are

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2})_{e}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Z}_{2}\text{ Trivial}=\text{Trivial}_{0}

(VI.143)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2})_{m}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\text{ SSB}=\text{Trivial}_{1}\oplus\text{Trivial}_{2}

Thus in total,
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
is a
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric (1+1)d gapped phase with three vacua. Again the question arises whether it is the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase or the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
SSB phase. The identification of the lines appearing in (
IV.159
) is now

\begin{aligned} 1_{ee}&\equiv 1_{00},\\
P_{ee}&\equiv 1_{00},\end{aligned}\,\quad\begin{aligned} 1_{mm}&\equiv 1_{11}\oplus 1_{22}\\
P_{mm}&\equiv 1_{12}\oplus 1_{21}\end{aligned}\,\quad\begin{aligned} S_{em}&\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{02},\\
S_{me}&\equiv 1_{10}\oplus 1_{20}\,.\end{aligned}

(VI.144)

This implies that the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry generators are identified as

\displaystyle 1_{-}

\displaystyle\equiv 1_{00}\oplus 1_{12}\oplus 1_{21}

(VI.145)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\equiv 1_{01}\oplus 1_{02}\oplus 1_{10}\oplus 1_{20}\oplus 1_{00}\,.

Note that there are
non-trivial relative Euler terms
between the three vacua now. To see this, note that the linking action (
V.104
) becomes

S_{em}:~v_{0}\to 2v_{1}+2v_{2}\,\qquad S_{me}:~v_{1}+v_{2}\to v_{0}\,,

(VI.146)

which means that the linking actions of the vacua changing lines are

\begin{aligned} 1_{01}&:~v_{0}\to 2v_{1},\\
1_{02}&:~v_{0}\to 2v_{2},\end{aligned}\,\qquad\begin{aligned} 1_{10}&:~v_{1}\to v_{0}/2\\
1_{20}&:~v_{2}\to v_{0}/2\end{aligned}

(VI.147)

implying that there are non-trivial relative Euler terms between vacua 0 and 1, and vacua 0 and 2, but trivial relative Euler terms between vacua 1 and 2. All these properties imply that
[\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{2}]
is the
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
SSB phase in which both
1_{-}
and
E
are spontaneously broken.

The
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
-symmetric phase transition
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
obtained after applying the KT transformation to the Ising phase transition can be expressed as follows. It comprises of two universes

(\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C})_{e}=\text{Ising}_{e}\,,\quad(\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C})_{m}=(\text{Ising}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{\sqrt{2}}=(\text{Ising}_{m})_{\sqrt{2}}\,,

(VI.148)

where we have used the well-known isomorphism of
\text{Ising}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
with Ising to express it as a copy
\text{Ising}_{m}
of Ising CFT. Note that there is relative Euler term between the two Ising universes with the linking action of universe changing lines

1_{em}:~\text{id}_{e}\to\sqrt{2}\text{id}_{m}\,\qquad 1_{me}:~\text{id}_{m}\to\text{id}_{e}/\sqrt{2}

(VI.149)

on the identity local operators
\text{id}_{e}
and
\text{id}_{m}
of
\text{Ising}_{e}
and
\text{Ising}_{m}
respectively. These linking actions reproduce (
V.104
) for lines
S_{em}
and
S_{me}
as these lines can be expressed as

\displaystyle S_{em}

\displaystyle=S_{ee}\otimes 1_{em}=1_{em}\otimes S_{mm}

(VI.150)

\displaystyle S_{me}

\displaystyle=S_{mm}\otimes 1_{me}=1_{me}\otimes S_{ee}\,,

where
S_{ee}
and
S_{mm}
are Kramers-Wannier duality defects of
\text{Ising}_{e}
and
\text{Ising}_{m}
respectively, whose quantum dimensions are
\sqrt{2}
, i.e.

S_{ee}:~\text{id}_{e}\to\sqrt{2}\text{id}_{e}\,,\qquad S_{mm}:~\text{id}_{m}\to\sqrt{2}\text{id}_{m}\,.

(VI.151)

The schematic form of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}
is thus

\text{Ising}_{e}\oplus(\text{Ising}_{m})_{\sqrt{2}}
1_{-}
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathcal{S}}_{C}\quad=
E
E

(VI.152)

The
\mathsf{Rep}(S_{3})
symmetry is realized as

1_{-}=1_{ee}\oplus\eta_{mm}\,,\quad E=S_{em}\oplus S_{me}\oplus\eta_{ee}\,,

(VI.153)

where
\eta_{ee}
and
\eta_{mm}
are the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetries of
\text{Ising}_{e}
and
\text{Ising}_{m}
respectively.

The relevant operator responsible for the transition is
\epsilon_{e}-\epsilon_{m}
. Adding this operator with positive sign sends
\text{Ising}_{e}
to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase and
\text{Ising}_{m}
to
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
